DIAGNOSTIC ACCURACY OF MRI IN DETECTING ROTATOR CUFF TEARS TAKING INDIRECT MR ARTHROGRAPHY AS GOLD STANDARD

DR. MARIA MURTAZA , DR MUHAMMAD AQEEL BABRI , DR. AFAF ARIF , DR. HASSAN RASHID , — 2026-12-15 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.18464498

Background: Rotator cuff tears are a common cause of shoulder pain and functional limitation. Accurate diagnosis is essential for appropriate management. While indirect MR arthrography (MRA) is considered the gold standard, plain MRI is widely used due to its non-invasive nature. Objectives: To determine the diagnostic accuracy of plain MRI in detecting rotator cuff tears, using indirect MR arthrography as the reference standard, and to evaluate its performance across different patient subgroups. Study Design & Setting: Cross-sectional validated study conducted at the Department of Radiology, Ittefaq Hospital Trust, Lahore from July 2025 to October 2025. Methodology: A total of 125 patients aged 2070 years with clinical suspicion of rotator cuff tear were enrolled using consecutive sampling. All patients underwent plain MRI followed by indirect MRA in the same imaging session. Images were independently interpreted by two experienced radiologists blinded to each others findings. Diagnostic performance of plain MRI was assessed using 2×2 contingency tables, calculating sensitivity, specificity, positive predictive value (PPV), negative predictive value (NPV), and overall accuracy. Stratification was performed by age, gender, and duration of symptoms. Results: Plain MRI detected 76/125 tears compared to 82/125 on indirect MRA. Overall sensitivity and specificity were 86.6% and 88.4%, respectively, with a PPV of 93.4%, NPV of 77.6%, and overall accuracy of 87.2%. Stratified analysis demonstrated high diagnostic performance across age, gender, and symptom duration subgroups, with statistically significant differences (p < 0.001). Conclusion: Plain MRI shows high diagnostic accuracy for detecting rotator cuff tears and may serve as a reliable non-invasive imaging modality, though indirect MRA remains the gold standard.

The Decoupling Mechanism as a Boundary Condition: Geometric Resolution to the Dark Sector and Hubble Tensions

Nadav Bashan — 2026-12-02 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.18675645

We test whether radiative decoupling surfaces admit a scale-independent geometric normalization that can be evaluated at stellar photospheres and projected to the cosmic horizon. By coupling independently measured radiative observables with Newtonian surface gravity, we define a dimensionless boundary invariant, \mathcal{X}. Using a benchmark sample of 190 detached eclipsing-binary (DEB) components, we find that stellar photospheres cluster near the projected blackbody phase-space capacity \mathcal{X}_0 = \pi^3/15 \simeq 2.067. While evaluating this invariant using standard macroscopic relations yields an algebraic tautology, this strict circularity mathematically demonstrates that standard physical laws inherently force mass and gravity to perfectly cancel at the radiative boundary, leaving a pure geometric invariant. Projecting this identical, mass-independent boundary normalization to the Hubble horizon yields a parameter-free prediction for the dark energy density fraction, \Omega_\Lambda = \pi^3/45 \simeq 0.6890, consistent with Planck constraints. Assuming a spatially flat universe, this boundary capacity strictly mandates a total matter density fraction of \Omega_m = 1 - \pi^3/45 \simeq 0.3110, seamlessly matching empirical consensus without fine-tuning. Finally, expressing the transition from a continuous early-universe fluid to a discrete late-universe void network as a geometric packing gap, motivated by the optimal local packing limit (k=12), yields a kinematic mapping H_0^{\mathrm{local}} = H_0^{\mathrm{CMB}}(13/12) \simeq 73.0\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}\,Mpc^{-1}}. This geometric formulation natively resolves the most persistent cosmological anomalies through pure spatial boundaries. Key Predictive Results This framework introduces a parameter-free, purely geometric formulation that natively resolves three of the most persistent anomalies in modern cosmology without the need for unobserved parameters or fine-tuning: Dark Energy (\Omega_\Lambda): Evaluated as a projected 2D horizon surface capacity rather than a 3D bulk volume density, yielding \Omega_\Lambda = \pi^3/45 \simeq 0.6890. Total Matter (\Omega_m): Derived directly from the spatial flatness constraint (\Omega_{\mathrm{tot}}=1) as the complementary geometric remainder, yielding \Omega_m = 1 - \pi^3/45 \simeq 0.3110. The Hubble Tension (H_0): Modeled as a kinematic offset reflecting the mandatory geometric packing gap between a continuous early-universe fluid and a discrete late-universe void network (based on the kissing number k=12), yielding H_0^{\mathrm{local}} = H_0^{\mathrm{CMB}}(13/12) \simeq 73.0 \mathrm{km\,s^{-1}\,Mpc^{-1}}. Repository Contents Manuscript.pdf: The full research paper. verify_boundary.py: A self-contained, reproducible Python script that performs the empirical validations and computes the cosmological projections. data.csv: The empirical benchmark dataset of 190 detached eclipsing binary (DEB) components from Torres et al. (2010) used for the photospheric evaluation.

The Holographic Boundary Principle: A Geometric Normalization from Stellar Photospheres to the Cosmic Horizon

Nadav Bashan — 2026-12-02 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.18723676

In this work, we present a geometric boundary framework that explicitly derives the gravitational relation (G) from the cosmological constant (Λ) through a scale-independent surface normalization. Starting from radiative decoupling surfaces, we identify a dimensionless boundary relation converging to the exact mass-independent identity π³/15. Extending this boundary condition to the cosmic horizon yields the master constraint ΛR_H² = π³/15. By incorporating holographic tiling of the horizon area, we extract an explicit algebraic expression for G, governed entirely by the discrete information bound (N) of the macroscopic surface. Within this formulation, the canonical ~ 10^120 vacuum-energy discrepancy is mathematically resolved not as an energy divergence, but as the exact dimensional scaling factor required to map the macroscopic horizon area to Planck-scale elements. Consequently, the dark-energy fraction and Hubble-scale kinematics emerge as rigid geometric imperatives of the identical boundary condition.

This repository contains the R code used for the statistical analyses reported in the manuscript entitled “Network Analysis of Depressive Symptoms, Resilience, and Illness Uncertainty in Patients with Primary Angle-Closure Glaucoma”The code includes data preprocessing, network estimation, centrality analysis, edge-weight accuracy assessment, and subgroup analyses by sex. The code is provided to facilitate transparency and reproducibility of the analyses reported in the manuscript.

WESTERN DIET AND GAMMA-IR, BUT NOT SIMGCRSIM, ARE ASSOCIATED WITH GREATER SEX-SPECIFIC LIVER CARCINOGENESIS IN AGING C57BL/6J MICE: code and data for reproducing the results

Suren Davitavyan, Arsen Arakelyan, David A. Goukassian — 2026-11-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22923475

This deposit contains the code and processed data needed to reproduce the transcriptomic and pathology analyses and figures associated with the manuscript "WESTERN DIET AND GAMMA-IR, BUT NOT SIMGCRSIM, ARE ASSOCIATED WITH GREATER SEX-SPECIFIC LIVER CARCINOGENESIS IN AGING C57BL/6J MICE" (Davitavyan et al.; corresponding author D. A. Goukassian), submitted to Science Advances. The study is a lifetime investigation of how diet and space-relevant ionizing radiation affect liver carcinogenesis in male and female C57BL/6J mice. At 90 days of age, animals received 100 cGy ¹³⁷Cs gamma irradiation (γ-IR) or 50 cGy simplified galactic cosmic ray simulation (simGCRsim) on a normal diet, or a Western diet for 125 days without irradiation. Livers were collected for bulk RNA-seq at 118, 455 and 530 days of age (89 samples after resolving duplicates across two sequencing runs: 45 male, 44 female). Animals were followed for neoplasm development to 750 days (males) and 640 days (females). The accompanying analysis covers RUVSeq normalisation and DESeq2 differential expression, self-organizing-map (oposSOM R package) transcriptomic portraits with spot-level functional enrichment, independent component analysis, gene set Z-score comparisons, TCseq temporal clustering, sex-biased and disease-related gene set mapping, and neoplasm incidence analysis. The deposit includes the R Markdown analysis script (Mice_Liver_Transcriptomics_Analysis.Rmd), input tables (STAR gene counts, sample metadata, RNA integrity numbers, gene set files), the trained SOM environment and complete oposSOM output, and the figures and result tables produced by the script. Analyses were run in R 4.4.2; package versions and instructions for running the script are listed in the README. Raw and processed sequencing data are available in NCBI GEO under accession GSE322758. This work was supported by NASA Human Research Program grants 80NSSC19K1079 and 80NSSC21K0549, American Heart Association Career Development Award 24CDA1269532, and Higher Education and Science Committee of the Republic of Armenia grants 24RL-1F029, 25FAST-1F004, 21AG-1F021 and 24AA-1F033.

TRACE: Deciphering Translation Grammar to Predict Translation Dynamics from RNA Sequences

Chunfu Xiao, Yao, Chao — 2026-10-21 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21469176

TRACE (Translatome Representation Across Cell Environment) is a Transformer-based model that decodes full-length transcriptomes into translatomes — predicting per-position ribosome occupancy profiles directly from RNA sequence and cellular context. TRACE integrates multi-omics data — transcript sequence, gene expression, and species identity — through adaptive layer normalization (AdaLN-Zero) to resolve translation regulation across cell types and species. Pre-processed training/validation/test H5 datasets and expression dictionariesare publicly available: > The archive contains `.train.h5` / `.valid.h5` / `.test.h5` files for each> species (human, macaque, mouse) as well as per-species expression dictionaries> (`{species}_expression_dict.pt`). ### H5 File Layout ``` .h5├── .attrs["n_samples"] int total number of transcripts├── .attrs["cell_type_counts"] JSON str cell-type distribution├── /cell_exprs/│ └── (d_expr,) Z-scored expression vector per cell type├── /sequences/│ └── (L, d_seq) continuous sequence features (e.g., one-hot codon)└── /samples/ / ├── .attrs["species"] str human | macaque | mouse ├── .attrs["cell_type"] str e.g., "heart", "liver", "HepG2" ├── .attrs["cds_start_pos"] int16 CDS start (1-based), -1 if unknown ├── .attrs["cds_end_pos"] int16 CDS end (1-based), -1 if unknown ├── .attrs["te_scale"] float32 translation efficiency (Z-scored), None if missing ├── .attrs["rpf_depth"] float32 ribosome profiling depth ├── .attrs["rpf_coverage"] float32 ribosome profiling coverage ├── .attrs["motif_occ"] list[int] upstream motif occurrences └── count_emb (L, d_count) per-position RPF density (target)``` ### Loading Data The `TranslationDataset` class provides lazy (recommended) or eager loadingfrom `.h5` files: ```pythonfrom data.translation_dataset import TranslationDataset # Lazy loading — minimal RAM, reads on-demand per __getitem__ds = TranslationDataset.from_h5("human.train.h5", lazy=True) print(f"Samples: {ds.n_samples}")print(f"Cell types: {ds.cell_type_counts}")print(f"Cell expr dict keys: {list(ds.cell_expr_dict.keys())}") # Access a single sample (returns one transcript)tid, species, cell_type, expr_vector, meta, seq_emb, count_emb = ds[0] print(f"species: {species}") # e.g., "human"print(f"cell_type: {cell_type}") # e.g., "heart"print(f"seq_emb shape: {seq_emb.shape}") # (L, d_seq)print(f"count_emb shape: {count_emb.shape}") # (L, d_count)print(f"cds: {meta['cds_start_pos']}–{meta['cds_end_pos']}")print(f"te_scale: {meta.get('te_scale')}") # Expression vector for a specific cell typeexpr_vec = ds.cell_expr_dict["heart"] # shape (d_expr,)```

Escaping the Enclave: Format-Faithful Synthetic Data for Open, Reproducible Medicare Claims Pipelines

Pavel Belakurski, D. Etin, Mark Chumack, Michael Bouzinier — 2026-10-19 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22803319

Developing and testing software for regulated patient data such as Medicare claims is structurally expensive. Tooling work must occur inside credentialed, audited computing enclaves, where libraries and tools require approval before they can even be tried. This makes pipeline development and debugging far slower and more expensive than on an ordinary open stack. Because derived code and data cannot leave the enclave, pipelines cannot easily be shared, compared, or reproduced by others. We hypothesize that escaping this bind does not require statistically realistic synthetic data. For the purpose of building and testing data tooling, a pipeline exercises file formats, record structures, schema conformance, edge cases, and data-quality artifacts, rather than the true magnitudes of clinical or demographic distributions. Format and structural fidelity are therefore sufficient to support these engineering tasks; statistical fidelity is not a prerequisite. This claim does not eliminate final validation on real data, both for substantive analyses and for behavior on unanticipated production edge cases.We make this case concrete with synthmed, an open (Apache-2.0) Python package that generates a fully synthetic Medicare-like database conforming to Research Data Assistance Center (ResDAC) File Transfer Summary (FTS) layouts, together with a downloadable companion dataset (Creative Commons Attribution 4.0, CC-BY-4.0; calendar years 2011-2016). The generator emits fixed-width files in the same layouts as real Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services (CMS) extracts, so the unmodified open dorieh ingestion pipeline can process them with no glue code and then run unchanged against the restricted extract inside the enclave. This demonstrates the approach for one workflow; transfer to other ResDAC consumers remains to be confirmed. Records are generated from AI-authored FTS specifications combined with public reference data and the CMS Data Entrepreneurs' Synthetic Public Use File (DE-SynPUF). An internal beneficiary table keeps synthetic identifiers and demographics consistent across files and years and injects data-quality artifacts to exercise error-handling and quality-control logic. No individual-level CMS ResDAC data or other real patient-level datasets are used, so the released files contain no protected health information or personally identifiable information.The generator is bit-reproducible under a fixed seed and has been run end to end with 5 million synthetic beneficiaries through the ingestion pipeline. An open quality-control run over that output identified a shape mismatch in the synthetic date-of-birth error distribution. Without the open synthetic run, detecting this deficiency would have required an authorized real-data run; the finding led to a corrected generation model.The artifacts support education, methods development, and extract-transform-load (ETL) or analysis-pipeline testing in population-health research. As more pipeline code is authored by AI, an open, format-faithful substrate of this kind also lowers the cost of independently inspecting, profiling, and regression-testing AI-generated ingestion logic outside the enclave. The released data are neither suitable nor intended for drawing substantive conclusions about real patients, providers, or health-care utilization.

転写ループを強制振動する概日 Ca²⁺振動体

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

飯塚　康介 — 2026-10-16 — openalex

DOI : —

時計遺伝子による概日転写回路は概日リズムの表出を担っている。この事実に基づき、概日時計の自律振動性は 40 年近くにわたって転写ループモデルで説明されてきた。しかし、この転写回路が実際に自律振動性を有するかは実験的証明が無い状況である。そして独立の研究チームから、転写回路が欠損した細胞においても概日リズムが報告され(O'Neill & Reddy, Nature, 2011; Ono et al., Nat. Commun., 2013)、振動の仕組みに関しては再考が求められている。私たちはこれまで、哺乳類の概日転写回路は Ca2+/カルモジュリン依存性プロテインキナーゼ II（CaMKII）や Na+/Ca2+交換輸送体（NCX）などの Ca²⁺シグナル因子によって制御されることを示してきた（Kon et al., Genes Dev.,2014; Sci. Adv., 2021）。そして、Ca²⁺機能の進化的保存性、および Cry1/2 欠損 SCN における Ca²⁺リズムの観察（Enoki et al., Sci. Rep., 2017）に基づき、概日転写回路の上 流には Ca²⁺リズムを駆動する祖先的な振動体が存在するのではと考えた。 まず、転写回路が破壊された状態でのリズムの性質を SCN において解析した。Bmal1欠損 SCN においても、概日 Ca²⁺リズムは不安定ながら自律振動性を示すことが分かった。また、Bmal1 欠損マウスにおける SCN の PER2 リズムは Ca2+依存的なリズムであること、および、概日時計の三大性質を示すことが分かった。 次に、HEK293A 細胞および低温下 Rat-1 細胞という、2 つの無リズム細胞モデルを解析した。これらのモデルにおいては、既知の時計遺伝子群が発現した状態で Ca2+および転写リズムが停止する。これらの細胞株において、24 時間周期の Ca²⁺刺激を人工的に誘導すると、Per2-Luc と Bmal1-Luc の逆位相リズムが駆動されることが分かった。そして、Rat-1 細胞を用いた時系列 RNA-seq 解析から、CaMKII 阻害下では転写回路の振動は停止することが分かった。これらの解析から私たちは、概日転写回路は自律振動性を有さず、上流の振動体が Ca²⁺-CaMKII シグナルを介して強制振動により駆動していると結論づけた。

Transcriptional and epigenetic changes associated with resistance to CDK4/6 inhibitors in HR+/HER2− breast cancer

Cristina Guarducci — 2026-10-16 — openalex

DOI : —

Background: CDK4/6 inhibitors (CDK4/6i), including palbociclib, ribociclib and abemaciclib, have revolutionized the treatment paradigm of hormone receptor positive and HER2 negative (HR+/HER2−) breast cancer. However, nearly all patients eventually develop resistance to these drugs. Although the mechanisms of resistance to CDK4/6i have been largely investigated, patient data indicate that resistance to these agents can be only partially explained by genetic alterations, suggesting that non-genetic mechanisms may contribute to the development of resistance. To date, the contribution of epigenetic alterations to the acquired resistance to CDK4/6i remains under-investigated. Methods: We performed an in vitro study to investigate the transcriptional, chromatin accessibility, and DNA methylation profile of HR+/HER2− MCF7 and T47D breast cancer cells by RNA-seq, ATAC-seq, and whole genome RRBS, after one week of palbociclib treatment and at the time of acquired resistance to palbociclib. Results: In both MCF7 and T47D cells, palbociclib induced coordinated changes in chromatin accessibility and transcriptional programs after short-term treatment and at the time of acquired resistance. Upon one week of palbociclib treatment, both models showed reduced proliferation and downregulation of cell-cycle-related genes, together with upregulation of epithelial–mesenchymal transition (EMT)-related genes and increased chromatin accessibility at sites enriched for AP-1 binding motifs. At the time of acquired resistance, resistant cells continued to show upregulation of EMT-related genes and exhibited increased expression of genes involved in estrogen receptor (ER) signaling, despite they showed reduced ER expression. Both resistant models showed distinct chromatin accessibility profiles compared with their parental untreated counterparts, with gained accessible sites still enriched for AP-1 binding motifs. AP-1 has been reported to function as a transcription factor that can promote EMT and facilitate ER binding to DNA. Interestingly, resistant cells remained sensitive to endocrine therapy agents targeting ER and showed increased sensitivity to AP-1 inhibition. While DNA methylation profiles were largely unchanged after short-term palbociclib treatment, resistant cells exhibited markedly different DNA methylation profiles compared with their parental counterparts, suggesting that the acquisition of a distinct DNA methylation profile is a specific feature of acquired resistance. However, no significantly enriched pathways were identified among the significantly deregulated genes associated with differentially methylated DNA regions. Conclusions: In HR+/HER2− cancer cells, palbociclib treatment and resistance result in transcriptional and epigenetic changes that might be driven by the transcription factor AP-1. Therapeutic strategies targeting the AP-1 axis could represent a potential approach to overcome CDK4/6i resistance and potentiate the effect of endocrine therapy in CDK4/6i-resistant cells. Based on our results, epigenetic alterations may provide novel biomarkers for the identification of CDK4/6i-resistant disease and inform the development of therapeutic strategies for resistant patients.

[Advances in early bedside etiological diagnosis of severe pneumonia].

C J Wei, Z S Cheng — 2026-10-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3760/cma.j.cn112147-20260519-00286

The case fatality rate of severe pneumonia remains high, early and accurate etiological diagnosis is a prerequisite for precision antimicrobial therapy. Conventional culture techniques are limited by long turnaround times and low positivity rates. In recent years, novel detection systems such as microbiological rapid on-site evaluation(M-ROSE), point-of-care immunoloassays, multiplex PCR, and metagenomic sequencing have advanced rapidly, markedly shortening the time to pathogen identification. The integration of rapid detection assays for antimicrobial resistance genes has further facilitated precision antimicrobial therapy. Multi-omics analysis integrating the pathogen spectrum with host immune response status helps distinguish colonization from infection, thereby helping prevent antibiotic overuse. This article reviews techniques for pathogen and antimicrobial resistance gene detection together with strategies to distinguish colonization from infection to inform rapid bedside etiological diagnosis in severe pneumonia.

PRIMAD-LID in Practice : An RO-Crate Profile for Reproducible ML in Bioinformatics

Meznah Aloqalaa, Nofe Ateq Alganmi, Stian Soiland‐Reyes, Carole Goble — 2026-10-06 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23104479

Machine learning reproducibility requires documentation of interdependent research components and the decisions made throughout a study. This poster presents ongoing work to develop an RO-Crate profile for representing reproducibility metadata in machine learning–based bioinformatics studies, guided by PRIMAD-LID and illustrated through scVI. PRIMAD-LID (Aloqalaa et al., 2026) is a discipline-diagnostic framework for computational reproducibility that retains the six PRIMAD dimensions (Freire et al., 2016): Platform, Research Objective, Implementation, Method, Actor and Data. Three cross-cutting modifiers qualify each dimension: Lifespan captures temporal considerations; Interpretation records contextual information and rationale; and Depth specifies the required level of detail and granularity. To establish the metadata foundation, source elements from ten selected reproducibility resources spanning multiple disciplines were mapped to PRIMAD-LID (Aloqalaa et al., 2026). Candidate metadata attributes were identified through direct extraction or analytical derivation and harmonised across disciplinary contexts. The proposed profile builds on this attribute set, using RO-Crate, a research packaging approach(Soiland-Reyes et al., 2022), to represent research arteficts and contextual metadata through entities, properties and relationships in a machine-readable form with human-readable views. An illustrative application to single-cell RNA sequencing (scRNA-seq) analysis using scVI (Lopez et al., 2018) targets data provenance, preprocessing and modelling choices, software dependencies, training parameters and outputs. References: Aloqalaa, M., Soiland-Reyes, S., & Goble, C. (2026). PRIMAD-LID: A developed framework for computational reproducibility [Preprint]. arXiv. DOI: 10.48550/arXiv.2601.02349. Freire, J., Fuhr, N., & Rauber, A. (Eds.). (2016). Reproducibility of data-oriented experiments in e-Science (Dagstuhl Seminar 16041). Dagstuhl Reports, 6(1), 108–159. DOI: 10.4230/DagRep.6.1.108. Gundersen, O. E., Cappelen, O., Mølnå, M., & Nilsen, N. G. (2025). The unreasonable effectiveness of open science in AI: A replication study. Proceedings of the AAAI Conference on Artificial Intelligence, 39(25), 26211–26219. DOI: 10.1609/aaai.v39i25.34818. Lopez, R., Regier, J., Cole, M. B., Jordan, M. I., & Yosef, N. (2018). Deep generative modeling for single-cell transcriptomics. Nature Methods, 15(12), 1053–1058. DOI: 10.1038/s41592-018-0229-2. Osborne, C., Ding, J., & Kirk, H. R. (2024). The AI community building the future? A quantitative analysis of development activity on Hugging Face Hub. Journal of Computational Social Science, 7(2), 2067–2105. DOI: 10.1007/s42001-024-00300-8. Packaging research artefacts with RO-Crate. Data Science, 5(2), 97–138. DOI: 10.3233/DS-210053.

An Integrated Approach to Cancer Therapy

Ashish Ranjan — 2026-10-05 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23076359

Abstract The convergence of therapeutic ultrasound, nanomedicine, immunotherapy, and comparative oncology provides new opportunities to develop cancer treatments with improved spatial control and systemic efficacy. Over the past two decades, a series of studies has investigated the use of focused ultrasound (FUS) not only for tumor ablation but also as a platform for image-guided drug delivery and immune modulation. Four interconnected areas have emerged from this work: (1) magnetic resonance-guided high-intensity focused ultrasound (MR-HIFU) for spatially controlled drug delivery; (2) nanoparticle approaches for enhancing immunogenic cell death; (3) focused ultrasound combined with intratumoral immunotherapy to generate local and systemic antitumor immunity; and (4) comparative oncology for translating these drug–device strategies into naturally occurring cancers. Collectively, these studies demonstrate an evolution from ultrasound-mediated local drug delivery toward integrated therapeutic platforms in which physical energy, nanotechnology, and immune modulation are combined to achieve both local tumor control and systemic antitumor responses. Comparative oncology provides an important translational bridge for evaluating these approaches under clinically relevant conditions and advancing promising drug–device combinations toward human cancer therapy. Keywords: focused ultrasound; HIFU; histotripsy; nanomedicine; immunotherapy; intratumoral therapy; comparative oncology; drug–device combinations Introduction Cancer is a biologically complex and heterogeneous disease shaped not only by malignant cells but also by their interactions with the surrounding tumor microenvironment. Genetic and phenotypic heterogeneity, abnormal vasculature, hypoxia, altered metabolism, immune suppression, and the capacity of tumor cells to adapt to therapeutic stress collectively contribute to treatment resistance and disease progression. These factors are particularly important in advanced and metastatic cancers, where effective treatment requires both local tumor control and elimination of disease beyond the primary tumor. Consequently, cancer treatment increasingly requires approaches that extend beyond individual therapeutic agents. Conventional systemic therapies can be limited by inadequate tumor penetration, off-target toxicity, and development of resistance, while local therapies may achieve effective control of the treated tumor without adequately addressing disseminated disease. Integrating physical energy, engineered drug-delivery systems, and immunotherapy provides an opportunity to address several of these biological barriers simultaneously. Focused ultrasound (FUS) is particularly attractive in this context because acoustic energy can be delivered noninvasively and spatially confined to defined tumor volumes. Depending on the exposure conditions, FUS can generate controlled hyperthermia, thermal ablation, or mechanical tissue disruption, while also influencing tumor vasculature, drug delivery, antigen release, and the immune microenvironment. Nanomedicine provides complementary capabilities by controlling the distribution, release, and biological activity of therapeutic agents. Together, these technologies provide a framework in which physical energy can determine where and when a therapeutic agent acts and, increasingly, how the tumor and immune system respond to treatment. Our research program has evolved across four interconnected areas: (1) image-guided focused ultrasound for spatially controlled drug delivery; (2) nanomedicine for enhancing immunogenic tumor-cell death; (3) focused ultrasound combined with intratumoral immunotherapy to generate local and systemic antitumor immunity; and (4) comparative oncology as a pathway for translating these drug–device strategies into naturally occurring cancers. 1. MR-HIFU-Guided Nanoparticle Drug Delivery An early objective was to determine whether focused ultrasound could provide spatial and temporal control of drug delivery. Ranjan, Jacobs, Woods, Negussie, Partanen, Yarmolenko, Gacchina, Sharma, Frenkel, Wood, and Dreher investigated clinical-grade doxorubicin-loaded low-temperature-sensitive liposomes in combination with a clinical MR-HIFU platform in rabbits bearing Vx2 tumors. MR-HIFU was used to generate controlled mild hyperthermia of approximately 40–41°C within the tumor following systemic administration of temperature-sensitive liposomes. The system achieved accurate and spatially homogeneous temperature control, allowing heat-triggered release of doxorubicin within the targeted tumor. The combination increased tumor drug delivery compared with the corresponding control approaches. This study demonstrated an important drug–device principle: focused ultrasound can serve as a noninvasive external trigger for localized pharmacologic activity. MRI simultaneously provides anatomical targeting and thermal monitoring, while temperature-sensitive nanoparticles provide a mechanism for converting a physical stimulus into localized drug release. The broader significance extends beyond doxorubicin. The approach established a framework in which therapeutic devices can regulate drug exposure in space and time, potentially increasing tumor concentrations while limiting unnecessary systemic exposure. Subsequent development of ultrasound-responsive delivery systems has continued to build upon this concept. 2. Nanomedicine and Immunogenic Cell Death The next phase of investigation extended the role of focused ultrasound from drug delivery to tumor immune modulation. An important limitation of many local cancer therapies is that destruction of malignant cells does not necessarily result in effective antitumor immunity. The nature of cell death and the associated danger signals can determine whether tumor destruction is immunologically silent or immunogenic. Calreticulin (CRT) is an important component of immunogenic cell death. Translocation of CRT to the tumor-cell surface can facilitate recognition and uptake of dying tumor cells by antigen-presenting cells. However, achieving predictable CRT expression within tumors remains challenging. Sethuraman, Singh, Patil, Li, Fiering, Hoopes, Guha, Malayer, and Ranjan therefore developed a calreticulin nanoparticle (CRT-NP) containing full-length CRT plasmid DNA and investigated its combination with focused ultrasound in melanoma. Intratumoral CRT-NP administration followed by FUS heating enhanced CRT expression and immunogenic cell death. The combination expanded melanoma antigen-specific functional CD4+ and CD8+ T-cell populations, promoted tumor-suppressive immune phenotypes, suppressed B16 melanoma growth, and inhibited growth at untreated distant tumor sites. These findings represented a conceptual transition from ultrasound-mediated drug delivery to ultrasound-assisted immune engineering. Nanoparticles were no longer used simply as carriers of cytotoxic drugs; they could deliver biological signals capable of altering how tumor death was recognized by the immune system. This work also established a foundation for integrating nanomedicine with other local therapies, including radiation, to enhance immunogenic cell death and antitumor immunity. 3. Focused Ultrasound and Intratumoral Immunotherapy Local administration of immunotherapeutics provides another strategy for manipulating the tumor microenvironment. Intratumoral delivery can generate high concentrations of immune-modulating agents at the tumor site while potentially limiting systemic exposure. Importantly, the tumor itself contains a diverse repertoire of patient-specific antigens and can potentially function as an in situ vaccine when tumor destruction is coupled with appropriate immune stimulation. Studies combining focused ultrasound heating with an agonistic CD40 antibody demonstrated that physical disruption or heating of tumors can be coupled with activation of antigen-presenting cells. In murine melanoma, this combination enhanced T-cell-mediated local and distant antitumor responses, supporting the concept that focused ultrasound can be integrated with intratumoral immune stimulation rather than functioning solely as an ablative technology. More recent work has extended this principle to histotripsy, a nonthermal focused-ultrasound technology that mechanically disrupts tumor tissue. Mechanical destruction is particularly interesting for immunotherapy because it can release relatively intact tumor-derived antigens. However, antigen release alone may be insufficient to overcome the immunosuppressive tumor microenvironment. Accordingly, biodegradable antigen-capturing particles incorporating immune-stimulatory signals have been developed to capture tumor material released following histotripsy while simultaneously promoting antigen-presenting-cell activation. Such strategies seek to transform mechanically disrupted tumor tissue into an individualized source of tumor antigens for vaccination. 4. Comparative Oncology and Translation of Drug–Device Combinations Translation of complex drug–device therapies presents substantial challenges. Murine models are essential for mechanistic investigation but cannot fully reproduce the biological heterogeneity, tumor size, treatment logistics, immune diversity, and longitudinal clinical management encountered in patients. Naturally occurring cancers in companion animals provide an important intermediate translational opportunity. Dogs and cats develop spontaneous cancers in the setting of natural aging, genetic diversity, environmental exposures, and intact immune systems. They can undergo advanced imaging, radiation therapy, therapeutic ultrasound, chemotherapy, biopsies, and serial immune monitoring using approaches similar to those employed in human oncology. The focused-ultrasound and nanomedicine program has therefore progressively incorporated naturally occurring canine cancers. Drug-delivery and immunot

Genomic characteristics and evolutionary analysis of Bordetella pertussis in Guizhou Province, China

Xuanjie Yang, Chunting Liu, Luming Yang, Ming Wang, MA Qing — 2026-10-05 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12866-026-05722-0

Abstract Background Pertussis is an acute infectious respiratory disease caused by Bordetella pertussis (Bp). Despite high vaccination coverage, the genome of B. pertussis is prone to variations. These variations can lead to vaccine immune escape, recurrence of outbreaks, and increased difficulty in prevention and control. Therefore, close monitoring of B. pertussis genotype characteristics and evolution is crucial for the precise prevention and control of pertussis. Methods In this study, whole-genome sequencing (WGS) was performed on 36 B. pertussis strains collected in Guizhou Province from 2017 to 2024. A systematic analysis was conducted regarding their antigen genotypes, Multiple-locus variable-number tandem repeat analysis (MLVA) molecular types, antimicrobial susceptibility, resistance genes and mutation sites, evolutionary dynamics, and pan-genome characteristics. Results From 2017 to 2018, the proportion of the Bordetella pertussis vaccine antigen sequence typing (BPagST) genotype BPagST4 ( ptxP 3/ ptxA 1/ ptxB 1/ ptxC 4/ ptxD 1/ ptxE 4/ fhaB -2400_5550-1/ fim 2-1/ fim 3-1) in Guizhou Province was 43.75%, while from 2023 to 2024, its proportion increased to 100%, becoming the dominant genotype. The proportion of prn 150 increased markedly from 0.00% (0/16) in 2017–2018 to 95.00% (19/20) in 2023–2024, becoming the dominant prn allele. MLVA analysis revealed a total of nine MLVA types among B. pertussis isolates in Guizhou, predominantly MT28 and MT195, of which MT28 accounted for 66.67% of the macrolide-resistant isolates. Among the 36 B. pertussis isolates, the resistance rate to macrolide antibiotics was 83.33%. All resistant isolates carried the 23S rRNA A2047G mutation and exhibited cross-resistance to macrolide antibiotics. Considerable divergence between the circulating isolates in Guizhou and the vaccine strains CS and Tohama I was revealed by phylogenetic analysis. Together with domestic and international strains, these isolates were clustered into three main clades. The Guizhou strains clustered closely with domestic and international strains in Clade 1 and Clade 2. All isolates in Clade 1 were assigned to BPagST4 and were mainly concentrated in the MT28/ ptxP 3/ prn 150 subclade. The 23S rRNA A2047G mutation was carried by all of them. Whole-genome analysis showed that the average nucleotide identity between the genomes of the Guizhou B. pertussis isolates and the reference vaccine strains (CS and Tohama I) exceeded 99.88%. Conclusion Between 2017 and 2024, the circulating B. pertussis isolates in Guizhou Province have exhibited clear trends of antigenic evolution and resistance evolution, forming a dominant macrolide‑resistant lineage of MT28/ ptxP 3/ prn 150, which is highly synchronized with the global epidemic trend of B. pertussis strains. This study provides an important molecular epidemiological basis for the precise prevention and control of pertussis, vaccine optimization, and rational clinical drug use in Guizhou Province.

Integrated Tick Management: Challenges, Opportunities and Future Perspectives

Pratik G. Kokate, Naina V. Singh, and Vishal B. Awate — 2026-10-05 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23139259

Ticks are important blood-feeding ectoparasites of livestock, companion animals and wildlife. In addition to causing direct damage and production losses, they transmit several pathogens responsible for important animal and zoonotic diseases. Chemical acaricides have traditionally been the principal method of tick control; however, their repeated use has resulted in the development of acaricide resistance and has raised concerns regarding environmental contamination, residues and toxicity. An integrated approach combining physical, chemical, biological and immunological methods can provide more sustainable tick management. Pasture management, entomopathogenic fungi, parasitoids, entomopathogenic nematodes, botanicals, host resistance and anti-tick vaccines are promising components of such an approach. Regular monitoring of acaricide resistance and rational use of chemicals, together with farmer awareness and adoption of alternative control measures, are essential for sustainable management of ticks and tick-borne diseases.

Associations between vaccine misinformation and influenza vaccine uptake: a population-based interrupted time-series study in China

Xiangting Zhang, Deng Xuan, Yuxia Liang, Hanqing He, Ying Wang — 2026-10-05 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s44482-026-00047-1

Abstract Vaccine misinformation is linked to vaccine hesitancy, yet real-world evidence of its association with vaccine uptake remains limited. Most existing studies rely on self-reported intentions or experimental designs, offering little insight into population-level behavioural changes. Using influenza vaccination data from Zhejiang, China, we examined changes in vaccination trends coinciding with three misinformation events. Interrupted time-series and counterfactual analyses were applied to trace changes in weekly vaccination rates by vaccine type (government-funded vs. self-paid), age group, and region. Significant downward shifts in vaccination trends followed two safety-related misinformation events during the vaccination season. During the Sinovac Vaccine Rumours of Pulmonary Nodules (2022), weekly trends shifted from a 4.8% increase before the event to a 1.8% increase after the transition period. A similar reversal was observed following the Pasteur Influenza Vaccine Lot Recall (2024) (from +7.3% to −9.0% per week). Government-funded vaccination rates appeared more consistently affected. In contrast, the administrative event ( Disciplinary Case of Former Chief Engineer of Sinopharm , 2024) showed no significant change. This study provides real-world evidence of an association between vaccine misinformation and influenza vaccination rates. Thematic and temporal patterns of misinformation events are associated with behavioural responses, highlighting the need for timely and responsive vaccine communication strategies.

Integrated pituitary adaptations to the low estrogen state

Tiffany K. Miles, Angela Katherine Odle, Ashley Herdman, Lillian G Clark, Stephanie D. Byrum — 2026-10-05 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3389/fendo.2026.1920998

Introduction Estrogen’s broad trophic effects on anterior pituitary function are not fully understood. The compound 4-vinyl cyclohexene diepoxide (VCD) induces early ovarian failure and reduced E2 production, which may differentially affect anterior pituitary cells. We recently identified transcriptomic changes in pituitary gonadotropes at the single-cell level in females treated with VCD for 20 days, which were acyclic, with low serum estradiol (E2) levels and 8-12-fold increases in gonadotropins 138 days later. Materials and methods We tested vehicle-control and VCD-treated female mice in metabolic cages and assayed serum hormone levels by EIA, and pituitary cell transcripts by qRT-PCR and single-cell RNA-seq. Results VCD-treated mice showed normal metabolic health. Compared with controls, VCD-treated females exhibited a 5-fold elevation in serum pituitary thyroid-stimulating hormone (TSH) levels and near-normal thyroid hormone levels. Pituitary Tshb mRNA levels were reduced, and pituitary Gh, Prl , and Pomc mRNA levels were unchanged. Serum growth hormone (GH) levels were higher in VCD-treated mice than in controls 62 days after the start of treatment (perimenopause) and then reduced to control levels after 118–138 days. Ingenuity Pathway Analyses (IPA) of upregulated differentially expressed gene (DEG) transcripts predicted activation of G protein-mediated signaling pathways in one scRNA-seq somatotrope subcluster and in the thyrotrope and lactotrope clusters. In contrast, downregulated DEGs in corticotropes and the remaining two somatotrope clusters predicted downregulation of critical signaling pathways. Discussion The unchanged pituitary Gh, Prl , and Pomc mRNA levels correlated with unchanged serum levels. The interim rise in serum GH 62 days after treatment agrees with reports of perimenopausal responses due to estrogen spikes. Reduced pituitary Tshb correlates with the recently reported reduced expression in gonadotropes. IPA analysis of upregulated DEGs in thyrotrope clusters predicted the activation of canonical signaling pathways that support elevated serum TSH levels. The near-normal thyroid hormone levels suggest subclinical hypothyroidism in menopausal women. Downregulated DEGs in corticotropes and in two somatotrope subclusters predicted compromised stress and metabolic responses. Finally, reduced multihormone gene expression suggested compromised plasticity in a low-estrogen environment. These different responses to a sustained low-estrogen state, such as that seen in menopause, reflect the diversity of estrogen’s trophic actions.

Targeting the CD4+ effector memory T cells re-expressing CD45RA suppressor galectin-7: a multi-omics-guided therapeutic strategy for idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis

Tao Yan, Jichang Liu, Yong Liu, Wenchao Gu, Jia Liu — 2026-10-05 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s43556-026-00542-0

Abstract Idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis (IPF) is a chronic, progressive interstitial lung disease with limited therapeutic options. Although single-cell and spatial transcriptomics have revealed cellular heterogeneity within the IPF lung microenvironment, the immune drivers with causal relevance remain poorly defined. Here, through large-scale Mendelian randomization integrating multi-omics data, we identified a consistent negative causal association between the proportion of CD4⁺ effector memory T cells re-expressing CD45RA (TEMRA) and IPF risk. Multi-platform validation—including single-cell RNA sequencing, flow cytometry, and multiplex immunofluorescence—confirmed a significant reduction in CD4⁺ TEMRA frequency in both peripheral blood and lung tissues of IPF patients, establishing this subset as a clinically relevant biomarker. Spatial transcriptomics further revealed a negative correlation between TEMRA differentiation signatures and local fibrotic burden. Mechanistically, integrated single-cell analyses pinpointed metaplastic KRT5⁺ basal cells as a key epithelial population with the capacity to disrupt immune homeostasis. These aberrant epithelial cells upregulate Galectin-7 ( LGALS7 ), which we demonstrate competes with IL-7 for receptor binding, suppresses STAT5 phosphorylation, and limits CD4⁺ TEMRA differentiation, thereby uncovering a previously unrecognized epithelial–immune crosstalk axis in IPF. Guided by these findings, virtual screening of 20,000 compounds identified neoeriocitrin as a Galectin-7 inhibitor that restores STAT5 phosphorylation in vitro and attenuates pulmonary fibrosis in vivo. This study positions Galectin-7 as a druggable target and establishes a novel framework linking epithelial dysfunction to immune dysregulation in IPF.

Shared genetic architecture underlying cataract and cardiovascular disease: insights from multi-omics analyses

Gangyi Li, Yuke Wang, Meng Ma, Shun Zeng, Wenjun Zhou — 2026-10-05 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-026-74417-3

Abstract Cataract and cardiovascular disease (CVD) are prevalent age-related disorders with overlapping risk factors. While epidemiological associations are established, the shared genetic mechanisms underlying their comorbidity remain largely unexplored. We performed a comprehensive genome-wide investigation integrating large-scale GWAS summary statistics for age-related cataract and four major CVDs (coronary artery disease, atrial fibrillation, heart failure, and myocardial infarction). Using multi-omics approaches including PLACO, MAGMA, SMR, and colocalization, we identified pleiotropic loci, credible genes, and evaluated their functional relevance. Pathway and gene-set enrichment analyses were conducted to elucidate the underlying biological mechanisms. Significant genetic correlations were found for cataract with AF, CAD, and HF (LDSC and HDL, Bonferroni-corrected P < 0.0125); MI showed method-dependent significance (LDSC P = 0.021, HDL P = 1.71 × 10 −4 ). PLACO identified 924 shared SNPs mapped to 79 loci, of which 12 showed robust colocalization (PP.H4 > 0.8). MAGMA gene-set enrichment revealed pathways related to lipid metabolism, oxidative stress, and vascular development. Gene-based analysis further stratified these into 50 credible genes supported by colocalization or SMR, including TOMM40, APOE, and ATPAF2. Functional enrichment showed these genes were enriched in LXR-mediated cholesterol metabolism, linking systemic and ocular pathophysiology. Our integrative multi-omics analysis reveals substantial genetic overlap between cataract and CVD, identifies pleiotropic genes, and uncovers shared molecular pathways. These findings provide candidate mechanistic hypotheses for the comorbidity of ocular and cardiovascular diseases and highlight candidate genes and pathways that warrant future functional validation.

Gut microbiota and serum metabolomics in children with human parainfluenza virus infection

Xinghan Ren, Zhuoying Liu, Liuxuan Huang, Xiaotong Lai, Binyu Wu — 2026-10-05 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3389/fcimb.2026.1941674

Objective Based on gut microbiota and serum untargeted metabolomics analyses, this study investigated changes in gut microbiota structure and serum metabolic profiles in children infected with human parainfluenza virus (HPIV). By integrating multi-omics data, we aimed to explore interactions between gut dysbiosis and serum metabolic disorders, and their potential roles in HPIV pathogenesis, to provide preliminary evidence for microbiota--targeted and metabolic intervention strategies. Methods Clinical data and laboratory results were collected from 230 hospitalized children (<14 years) with HPIV infection. Subsequently, 31 HPIV-infected children and 12 healthy controls were enrolled. Fecal samples were analyzed via 16S rRNA gene sequencing, and serum samples via liquid chromatography-tandem mass spectrometry (LC-MS/MS)-based untargeted metabolomics, followed by integrated multi-omics analysis. Results Children with severe HPIV infection had longer hospital stays and higher frequencies of wheezing, tachypnea, dyspnea, and vomiting. HPIV infection was associated with gut dysbiosis: opportunistic pathogens ( Anaerococcus , Peptoniphilus , Finegoldia ) were enriched, while beneficial commensals ( Blautia , Bifidobacterium , Faecalibacterium ) were reduced. Metabolomics identified 579 upregulated and 211 downregulated metabolites, primarily enriched in alpha-linolenic acid, butanoate, glycerophospholipid, starch/sucrose, and glycine/serine/threonine metabolism pathways. Correlation analysis showed that enriched opportunistic pathogens positively correlated with pro-inflammatory lipids (e.g., acetoacetic acid, 3-hydroxybutyric acid) and negatively with protective metabolites (e.g., alpha-linolenic acid, sucrose, L-threonine), while Blautia showed opposite patterns. The four key genera and five metabolites demonstrated exploratory discriminatory capacity for HPIV infection (AUC: 0.81 and 0.98, respectively), serving as candidate biomarkers for further validation. Conclusion HPIV infection may be associated with gut microbiota dysbiosis and serum metabolic disturbances in children, potentially via the gut–lung axis, and these alterations may be involved in modulating pulmonary antiviral immune and inflammatory responses. This multi-omics study provides preliminary evidence for future research on prevention and treatment strategies targeting the gut microbiota or metabolic pathways in children with HPIV infection.

A CCL21 gene-modified dendritic cell vaccine combined with pembrolizumab in advanced non-small cell lung cancer: a phase I trial

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Michael S. Oh, Aaron Elliott Lisberg, Camelia Dumitras, Linh My Tran, Ramin Salehi‐Rad — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-026-78130-7

Abstract Immune checkpoint inhibitors (ICIs) have transformed treatment for non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC), but resistance to these therapies is common. We report here results from a phase I trial combining intratumoral injection of a CCL21-expressing dendritic cell (CCL21-DC) vaccine with pembrolizumab in patients with advanced non-small cell lung cancer (ClinicalTrials.gov identifier: NCT03546361). The primary objectives were to determine the maximum administered dose (MAD) in the dose escalation phase and the objective response rate (ORR) in the dose expansion. Eleven patients were enrolled in the dose escalation cohort, and 12 patients were treated in the dose expansion cohort at the MAD of 3 ×10 7 cells/injection. The trial did not meet its primary endpoint for ORR as no objective responses were observed, though 36.8% of patients achieved a best response of stable disease. The combination therapy was well tolerated. Post-hoc analyses of longitudinal biopsies revealed that the therapy increased tumor infiltration by CD4+ T cells and novel T cell clones. Disease stability was associated with increased T cell receptor diversity and greater expansion of novel clones following therapy. However, many novel clones failed to persist over time, and immunosuppressive myeloid signaling signatures were identified across time points. Our findings overall demonstrated that CCL21-DC vaccination combined with pembrolizumab can promote T cell infiltration, but this did not translate into meaningful clinical efficacy, potentially due to limited tumor specificity of the T cell response and an inability to fully reprogram the tumor microenvironment.

Which Countries Require Travel Vaccines? Yellow Fever Entry Rules, Malaria Prevention, Vaccine Coverage and Cases from WHO International Travel and Health Data, with U.S. Travel Advisory Levels and Travel Vaccine Prices, 1980–2026

Marcus Oyelaran, Daniel R. Whitcombe — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23146904

Under the International Health Regulations, yellow fever is the only vaccination a country may routinely require for entry. We parse the World Health Organization's International Travel and Health Country List and Annex 1 (2022 edition, revised January 2023) entry by entry and combine them with Global Health Observatory series, the U.S. travel advisory feed of September 2026 and published vaccine prices. Of 220 countries and territories, 119 (54.1%) required yellow fever proof from some travellers: 100 from arrivals from risk countries and 19, all but one in Africa, from every traveller. The share ranged from 92.0% of entries in the African region to 5.4% in Europe. Only 37 of the 119 were themselves among the 42 places at risk of transmission, and five at-risk places required nothing. Most thresholds were 1 year (61) or 9 months (54) of age, and 59 of 100 arrival-based rules counted transits of more than 12 hours. The list is dated: only 70 of 196 States Parties (36%) answered the 2022 survey, none of 47 in Africa, and 65.0% of entries were last confirmed in 2021 or 2022. Type C malaria prevention applied to 75 entries. Yellow fever vaccine coverage among 1-year-olds in countries at risk rose from 12% in 2000 to 53% in 2025, against an 80% target, while reported cases rose three years running to 631 in 2025, led by the Americas. In September 2026, 24 of 214 destinations were at U.S. advisory Level 4, including 7 of the 19 all-traveller yellow fever places. Proposed county clinic fees put a yellow fever dose at USD 222 and common vaccine sets at USD 287 to USD 994.

Recent advances in universal influenza vaccine platforms: paving the way for universal H5N1 vaccines and pandemic readiness

Safdar Ali, Humaira Bibi, Hikmat Ullah, Muhammad Shahid Mehmood, Mohammad Azhar Kamal — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s44368-026-00036-9

Abstract The H5N1 influenza threat is a significant issue to the overall health of the population in the world, and it demands new approaches in vaccine development and implementation. The critical path forward is the development of universal vaccine platforms, which provide long-term cross-protection against various H5N1 strains. This type of vaccines, based on conserved regions of viruses, is necessary to provide long term immunity against antigenic variation. The future of vaccines should be based on the ability to generate both cellular and humoral immunity and provide the greatest level of protection against a variety of viral variants. It is also crucial that agile manufacturing capacities are established, which can swiftly expand the production of vaccines to satisfy the demand in the world in the case of an influenza outbreak. The technology of mRNA vaccines, viral vectors, or microneedle patches can be adapted to novel strains rapidly and responds effectively to the situation when rapid and effective responses are required. Also, fair global distribution policies can be crucial to make it possible to make vaccines available to the population of low- and middle-income countries. The international response needs to focus on equity, whereby inequality in access to vaccines is minimized. This has been the ultimate solution of combining universal vaccines, cross-protection, agile production, and equity over the world to reduce the threat of H5N1 pandemic. By developing these measures, the world community can be more ready to the upcoming outbreak of influenza, and access to a vaccine can be timely, extensive, and efficient.

Evaluation of the immunogenic properties of a chimeric protein against West Nile virus: in-silico analysis and assessment in mouse

Mahdi Arezoumandi, Sadegh Shojaei Baghini, Nima Rad, Elham Taghipour, Mohammad Hassan Pouriayevali — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.18502/ijm.v18i5.22876

Background and Objectives: West Nile virus (WNV) is a mosquito-borne flavivirus that represents a significant public health concern, and the development of effective vaccine candidates remains a priority. This study aimed to design a chimeric multi-epitope protein against WNV using immunoinformatics approaches and evaluate its immunogenicity in mice. Materials and Methods: Selected regions of the WNV envelope (E) and non-structural 1 (NS1) proteins were analyzed to identify B- and T-cell epitopes using immunoinformatics tools. The selected epitopes were incorporated into a chimeric con- struct fused with cholera toxin B subunit, and its antigenicity, allergenicity, structural properties, and TLR3 interaction were evaluated in silico. The optimized construct was expressed in Escherichia coli, purified by Ni-NTA affinity chromatography, and confirmed by SDS-PAGE and western blotting. BALB/c mice were immunized with the recombinant protein plus adju- vant, and immune responses were assessed using ELISA, cytokine assays, and flow cytometry. Results: Immunized mice developed high IgG antibody titers and showed detectable IFN-γ and IL-5 production, suggesting induction of Th1-associated responses with limited Th2-related cytokine production. The chimeric protein also stimulated CD4+ and CD8+ T-cell responses, indicating activation of both humoral and cellular immunity. Conclusion: The designed chimeric protein showed immunogenic potential and induced humoral and cellular immune re-sponses in mice, supporting further evaluation as a candidate for WNV vaccine development.

Validation of the behavioural and social drivers of influenza vaccine survey in Australia: Cognitive interviews and psychometric evaluation

Majdi M. Sabahelzain, Maria Christou-Ergos, Julie Leask — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.34064157

Seasonal influenza vaccination reduces the risk of contracting influenza. In Australia, vaccination coverage is consistently low among the most at-risk groups eligible for the free influenza vaccine. To establish an annual national monitoring of the behavioral and social drivers of influenza vaccination, we validated the World Health Organization’s Behavioral and Social Drivers of Influenza Vaccination survey. Ours is the first study reporting the validation results of the newly developed survey tool. Two validation stages occurred: first, cognitive interviews and survey item revisions based on feedback; second, collecting quantitative data, performing psychometric analysis, including factor analysis, and assessing the association between survey items and influenza vaccination intention using polychoric correlation and a random forest model to determine each item’s importance. We modified nine of the 31 survey items based on cognitive interviews with 16 adults. Based on the psychometric evaluation of 2055 respondents, we identified and validated five factors that underlie the survey structure and aligned well with the four domains of the BeSD framework: Thinking and Feeling, Social Processes, Motivation, and Practical Issues. Each item correlated with the intention to receive influenza vaccine, particularly those related to perceived vaccine importance and social responsibility. We recommend further validation in various contexts, especially to assess the survey’s predictive validity regarding actual vaccination uptake.

PELATIHAN EXTENDED e-iANC: PENINGKATAN MANAJEMEN DATA VAKSIN BIDAN IBI JAKARTA BARAT

Hosizah Hosizah, Tria Saras Pertiwi, Bambang Irawan, Naia Putri Ramadhani, Syalaisha Nuraini Rachmadiany — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.31764/jmm.v10i5.42221

Abstrak: Program imunisasi efektif mencegah PD3I, namun pengelolaan cold chain vaksin di TPMB IBI Cabang Jakarta Barat masih manual berbasis kertas sehingga menyulitkan pemantauan suhu dan stok vaksin secara real-time. Kegiatan pengabdian kepada masyarakat ini bertujuan untuk meningkatkan pengetahuan dan keterampilan bidan dalam menerapkan digitalisasi manajemen data vaksin melalui aplikasi Extended e-iANC. Tujuh orang bidan dari enam TPMB dilatih menggunakan Extended e-iANC pada fitur ”Manajemen Data Vaksin”. Kegiatan diselenggarakan di Universitas Esa Unggul pada tanggal 18 Juni 2026, mencakup penyampaian materi, demonstrasi aplikasi, dan sesi praktik, dengan efektivitas pelatihan dievaluasi menggunakan desain one-group pretest-posttest sebanyak 20 soal, serta dianalisis menggunakan uji Wilcoxon Signed Rank Test (α=0,05). Hasil kegiatan menunjukkan rata-rata skor pengetahuan peserta meningkat dari 88,57 ± 11,44 pada saat pretest menjadi 98,57 ± 3,78 pada saat posttest, dengan nilai p sebesar 0,027 (p < 0,05), yang menunjukkan peningkatan pengetahuan yang signifikan setelah pelatihan. Penurunan standar deviasi juga mengindikasikan bahwa pemahaman peserta menjadi lebih merata. Pelatihan berbasis praktik ini terbukti efektif dalam meningkatkan kesiapan bidan untuk mengadopsi teknologi digital guna mendukung pengelolaan data vaksin yang lebih akurat dan terintegrasi di TPMB.Abstract: Immunization programs are a key public health intervention for preventing vaccine-preventable diseases, yet cold chain vaccine management at Independent Midwifery Practices (TPMB), including those affiliated with the Indonesian Midwives Association (IBI) West Jakarta Branch, is still recorded manually on paper, making real-time monitoring of vaccine temperature and stock difficult. This community service activity aimed to improve midwives' knowledge and skills in digitalizing vaccine data storage using the Extended e-iANC application. Seven midwives from six TPMB were trained on the “Vaccine Data Management” module of the Extended e-iANC system. The event was held at Universitas Esa Unggul on June 18, 2026. The training included material delivery, application demonstration, and practical sessions, with effectiveness evaluated using a one-group pretest-posttest design and the Wilcoxon Signed Rank Test (α = 0.05). Results showed the participants' average knowledge score increased from 88.57 ± 11.44 at pretest to 98.57 ± 3.78 at posttest, with a p-value of 0.027 (p < 0.05), indicating a significant improvement in knowledge following the training. The decreased standard deviation also indicated more uniform understanding among participants. This practice-based training effectively improved midwives' readiness to adopt digital technology to support more accurate and integrated vaccine data management at TPMB.

The tRNA‐Modification Geranyl‐2‐Thiouridine Enables RNA‐Lipid Association

Johannes Singer, Kathrin Halter, Chun‐Yin Chan, Thomas Carell — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1002/anie.9140489

The packaging of RNA in lipid nanoparticles for protection and delivery was key for the development of mRNA vaccines and therapeutics. Formulation requires the addition of a variety of lipids (including cationic) in high amounts to form complexes with the negatively charged RNA. While this approach was successful for the development of RNA vaccines, it represents a major challenge for future developments. Increasing the lipophilicity of RNA is one possible approach to reducing the amount of lipid required. In nature, geranyl-2-thiouridine occurs at the "Wobble" position 34 of certain bacterial tRNAs. This work details investigations into geranylated oligonucleotides and their interactions with lipids. We developed an efficient post-synthetic procedure for oligonucleotide geranylation in aqueous solutions using a geranyl-sulfonium salt. We discovered that the geranyl-modification can be replaced by thiol-nucleophiles, enabling a reversible switch of the hydrophobic character of the oligonucleotides. We show that the geranyl-modified oligonucleotides anchor onto neutral membrane structures and oil droplets. Oligonucleotides bound to the outer surface of neutral liposomes can flip to the inner membrane surface by freeze-thaw cycles, leading to a protective effect against enzymatic or chemical degradation. Our work expands the scope of oligonucleotide lipidation strategies, which are of great interest in RNA delivery systems.

Root Causes of Childhood Vaccination Decline in Bosnia and Herzegovina: An AI-Assisted Scoping Review

Mia Blažević, Priyasha Maharjan, Benjamin, Mohammad Gazi Shah Alam — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.17605/osf.io/325ny

The landscape of global childhood immunization has experienced unprecedented volatility, accelerated by the COVID-19 pandemic. Bosnia and Herzegovina currently exhibits high rates of zero-dose and under-vaccinated children within the European and Central Asian regions. Routine vaccination coverage across the Federation of Bosnia and Herzegovina, Republika Srpska, and the Brčko District has maintained a downward trajectory. National first-dose measles coverage (MCV1) was reported at 60.2% in 2024, persisting well below the 95% threshold required for population herd immunity. This decline has precipitated recurrent outbreaks of vaccine-preventable diseases. This scoping review systematically maps the temporal and geographical trends of routine childhood immunization coverage and multi-dose attrition in Bosnia and Herzegovina from 2005 to 2026. The investigation utilizes the World Health Organization’s Behavioral and Social Drivers of Vaccination (BeSD) framework to categorize documented barriers. By disaggregating these root causes into cognitive, social, motivational, and practical domains, the review isolates systemic health delivery frictions from psychosocial resistance . The methodology adheres to the JBI Manual for Evidence Synthesis and PRISMA-ScR reporting guidelines. A robust search strategy interrogates major academic databases alongside targeted regional grey literature from domestic public health institutes. The study integrates a Human-in-the-Loop (HITL) Large Language Model extraction protocol to verify quantitative epidemiological metrics and qualitative narratives with high structural accuracy. Data synthesis involves descriptive time-series trend mapping and deductive thematic coding executed within ATLAS.ti. The expected outcomes include a descriptive epidemiological analysis and a visual Evidence Gap Map. This map will plot identified BeSD barriers against tested public health mitigation strategies within the Western Balkans. The synthesis will quantify the operational efficacy of specific interventions in analogous transitional health systems. The final analysis aims to identify actionable, evidence-based programmatic solutions capable of closing existing immunity gaps and restoring routine immunization uptake.

Statistical inference of virus evolution using ancestral recombination graphs

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Christopher Leung, Charlotte J. Houldcroft, Aylwyn Scally — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.10.02.756209

Ancestral recombination graphs (ARGs) represent the coalescent history at every locus in the genome, subject to mutation and recombination. Modelling viruses using the coalescent with recombination remains less explored, due to the potential for complex, multi-scale dynamics. Using perturbative methods, we establish mathematical bounds that describe the balance between inter-host and within-host coalescence, and demonstrate that the highly-recombinant Epstein-Barr Virus (EBV) coalesces predominantly at the inter-host level. We then modify SINGER, a tool from diploid genetics, for haploid ARG inference with fine-grained variable recombination rates. We provide insight into the paradox of high recombination rates and slow linkage disequilibrium decay, as ARG-based methods potentially allow us to disentangle epistatic effects from shared ancestry, and illustrate this using the coalescent topologies of EBV's host cell-entry glycoproteins. Our findings demonstrate that ARG-based methods recover gene-level selective structure, and provide a general method for analysing virus phylodynamics in the limit of small quantities.

Source data for: CXCR3 on transferred T cells enables vaccine-boosted adoptive T-cell therapy in solid tumors

Vinnycius Pereira Almeida, Lucy C. Garner, Sara Abdel Malak, Amanda Wicki, James McAuliffe — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23134889

Underlying numerical source data for every figure panel of Pereira Almeida et al., covering main figures 1 to 5 and supplementary figures S1 to S10. The record contains the same source data in two forms: data_S1.xlsx — a multi-sheet workbook with one worksheet per figure (Fig1 to Fig5, FigS1 to FigS10), plus a README worksheet describing the conventions and a Panel index worksheet listing every panel, the number of tables provided and the statistics reported. Within each worksheet the panels appear in order under a labelled header, followed by the individual measurements plotted, one row per animal, cell or replicate, one column per group or timepoint, and the statistical output as computed, giving the test name, the test statistic, the degrees of freedom where applicable, and the exact P value. Survival panels give the animal identifier, the time to event, and an event or censoring indicator. data_S1.zip — the same data as 2,268 individual CSV files in 117 per-panel folders, for machine reading. 134 panels are catalogued in total; 17 are experimental schematics or representative flow cytometry plots with no underlying quantification and are listed as such. 1,204 P values are recorded. The data and analysis tables were extracted directly from the original GraphPad Prism files, which store each table as plain CSV. Nothing was recomputed, so the deposited values cannot drift from the published figures, and Prism's own analysis output is preserved with its row labels. Column titles were reconstructed from Prism's dataset metadata and written only where the reconstructed width matches the file, so a column can never be silently misaligned against its label; 874 of the 875 source tables carry full headers. Analysis code that regenerates the figures from these data is archived separately at doi:10.5281/zenodo.22655495.

Characterizing the B-cell senescence signature in driving the disease phenotype in COPD/emphysema

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Gagandeep Kaur, Qixin Wang, Felix Effah, Sadiya Bi Shaikh, Calla Fahey — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s43856-026-01928-5

Increased cellular senescence is a key mechanism underlying chronic inflammation and airway remodeling in chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD). Yet, the cellular composition and molecular features of the senescent microenvironment in COPD remain poorly understood. To address this gap, we employed a multi-omics approach to identify senescence-associated differentially regulated cell populations and associated genes in smoke-induced emphysema. Single-cell RNA sequencing analyses of 121,885 lung cells from COPD patients and healthy controls (n = 6 per group), including individuals with (Sm) and without (NS) a smoking history, were analyzed using standard approaches. Our analyses reveal a significant enrichment (p < 0.05) of senescence-associated gene signatures within B-cell clusters from COPD lungs. The transcriptional profile of senescent B cells include: (a) impairment of B cell differentiation and maturation (KITLG, PRDM1), (b) increase in inflammatory signaling (CXCL3, IL6, GDF15), and (c) dysregulation of lipid metabolism (PTGS2), thereby releasing factors affecting ECM composition (MMP14) and cell-cell interactions in COPD. Spatial transcriptomics and proteomics validate the scRNA seq findings. We identify distinct ‘senescent’ niches within COPD lungs that shows persistent inflammatory signaling, activation of p16-mediated senescence, and dysregulation of intercellular communication. Notably, we report a shift toward ADGRE5-CD55 ligand–receptor interactions between B lymphocytes and alveolar type II (AT2) cells in senescent regions in COPD donor tissues. Together, these findings identify and characterize B-cell senescence as a feature of the COPD lung microenvironment providing novel insight into the mechanistic underpinning of pathophysiology in COPD. Premature lung aging remains a well-known feature in smokers with Chronic Obstructive Pulmonary Disease (COPD). While previous attempts have been made to design therapies to reverse the smoking-induced lung aging, progress in this regard remains challenging. Here, we use modern state-of-the-art techniques to identify the cell population playing a central role in affecting premature lung aging during disease development amongst chronic smokers. Successful validation of our study findings was performed using fresh lung tissues and blood plasma from healthy and COPD patients to identify possible cell types and biomolecules that could be targeted as therapies in future for this debilitating disease. Kaur et al., profile senescence-associated cell populations in COPD using single-cell RNA sequencing of 121,885 lung cells, supported by spatial transcriptomics and proteomics, to define the senescent lung microenvironment. Senescent B cells are enriched in COPD lungs and exhibit inflammatory, differentiation, and lipid metabolism abnormalities that define distinct senescent niches with altered cell-cell communication, including enhanced ADGRE5-CD55 signaling between B cells and alveolar type II cells.

NFKB–P53 Rheostat Analysis Pipeline (R)

Spiros Vlahopoulos — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.19552321

NFKB–P53 Rheostat Analysis Pipeline (R) This record accompanies version 1.0 of the NFKB–P53 Rheostat Analysis Pipeline, an open-source R implementation of the rheostat model described in "The NFKB-P53 antagonism as part of a rheostat network: practical implication in solving the leukemia relapse principle" (Spiros Vlahopoulos, ORCID 0000-0001-6245-3863). The pipeline operationalizes the central abstraction of that work — that the antagonism between the inflammatory transcription factor complex NFKB and the tumor suppressor P53 can be collapsed into a single interpretable axis — as executable research code. It implements all eleven tasks of the article's proposed outline: definition of curated NFKB-activated and P53-activated gene sets (with helpers to fetch full MSigDB Hallmark sets), ingestion of bulk or single-cell RNA-seq data, per-sample scoring of both programs via ssGSEA or a transparent mean-of-z method, computation of the rheostat value (Rheostat = NFKB score − P53 score), visualization of sample positions along the axis, statistical comparison across clinical states such as diagnosis, remission, and relapse, association testing with survival and minimal residual disease endpoints, robustness checks across upstream drivers (mutations, infections, radiation, inflammatory triggers), optional integration of multi-omic layers such as ATAC-seq and proteomics, and derivation and evaluation of a simple high-risk decision rule. A complete simulated leukemia cohort allows the entire pipeline to run end-to-end without any data, so that users can verify their installation and learn the workflow before substituting their own datasets. The release consists of the pipeline script together with a beginner's guide that requires no prior R experience, covering installation, execution, adaptation to real data, and troubleshooting. The code is provided for research and exploratory purposes only: the rheostat is a functional readout intended to support biological insight and hypothesis generation, it summarizes downstream state and does not itself establish causality, and it is not intended to replace clinical judgment or to serve as the sole basis for diagnosis or treatment decisions. Any clinical application requires rigorous external validation, prospective evaluation, and regulatory review appropriate to the intended context of use. The gene lists shipped by default are indicative subsets and should be replaced with fully curated, published gene sets for any real analysis. This software and its accompanying documentation were prepared with the assistance of Mistral AI (Vibe) and are released under the MIT License; users are encouraged to cite this record when reusing or adapting the pipeline, and to report issues or extensions via the associated repository.

Figure-reproduction code and raw data for "Polyamines regulate cell fate by altering histone tail dynamics"

Source code and raw data to reproduce all main and Extended Data figures of the manuscript “Polyamines regulate cell fate by altering histone tail dynamics” (Emmons-Bell et al.). Data types include ATAC-seq and RNA-seq summary tables, LC-MS/GC-MS metabolomics, flow cytometry (FCS and gated GatingSets), confocal and immunofluorescence microscopy, western blots, and reanalyses of published OSKM reprogramming datasets (Chronis et al. 2017; Schiebinger et al. 2019). Scripts require R 4.5.x and run in a UTF-8 locale; see the top-level README for the full package list and a run_all.sh convenience script.
