PTTG1 drives intestinal-type gastric cancer progression via G2/M checkpoint-mediated cell-cycle reprogramming

胡采红, Lijun Bian, Lin Luo, Zhonghua Zheng, Yuheng Chen — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.isci.2026.117742

Intestinal-type gastric cancer (IGC) progresses through the Correa cascade, yet the molecular determinants of this transition remain insufficiently defined. Using single-cell RNA sequencing (scRNA-seq) on matched normal, intestinal metaplasia, and tumor tissues from five patients, we identified eight epithelial subtypes, including a transitional enterocyte population that expanded along the disease trajectory. Pseudotime and network analyses revealed a progressive upregulation of PTTG1 , which was further supported by public cohorts, bulk transcriptomics, and tissue microarrays. Functional assays demonstrated that PTTG1 knockdown impaired G2/M checkpoint regulation and reduced gastric cancer cell proliferation, migration, and invasion, whereas its overexpression enhanced organoid growth and altered mucin-related features. In xenograft models, PTTG1 depletion suppressed tumor growth and was accompanied by secretory differentiation-associated histological changes. Collectively, these findings suggest that PTTG1 contributes to intestinal-type gastric cancer progression by regulating G2/M checkpoint-associated programs and modulating mucin-associated phenotypes, supporting its potential as a risk biomarker and candidate therapeutic target for IGC prevention.

IL-15 drives antigen-independent increase in granzyme K+B+ CD8 T cells enriched in rheumatoid arthritis joints

Takahiro Natori, Hisakata Yamada, Ryosuke Tsurui, Yukio Akasaki, Shinya Kawahara — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jaut.2026.103640

OBJECTIVES: CD8 T cells co-expressing granzymes K and B (GzmK + B+) are enriched in the joint compared with peripheral blood and have been implicated in the pathogenesis of rheumatoid arthritis (RA). However, it remains unclear whether the enrichment of GzmK+B+ subsets is specific to RA. In addition, the mechanism underlying the generation of GzmK + B + CD8 T cells remains unknown. METHODS: Characteristics of CD8 T cells in the synovium of patients with RA and osteoarthritis (OA) were examined by a flow cytometer. Public database of single cell RNA sequence (scRNA-seq) of RA and OA synovium was analyzed, and the results were verified by in vitro culture experiments. Flow cytometric analysis of viral antigen-specific CD8 T cells identified by MHC class I tetramer was performed. RESULTS: GzmK+B+ subset in CD8 T cells was more enriched in RA than OA joint, whereas no difference was observed in the frequency of GzmK + B- subset. In silico analysis of public database recapitulated these results and suggested the transition from GzmK + B- to GzmK+B+ subset which is mediated by IL-15- but not TCR-signaling. IL-15 induced an increase in GzmK + B + CD8 T cells from RA and OA joints in vitro. Viral antigen specific bystander CD8 T cells in RA joints are also enriched of GzmK+B+ subset. CONCLUSIONS: Our results suggest that increased IL-15 in RA joints promotes bystander activation of CD8 T cells, leading to the enrichment of GzmK + B + CD8 T cells, which are implicated in the pathogenesis of RA.

Code for:Polygenic risk for schizophrenia is associated with alternative polyadenylation as molecular mechanism contributing to synaptic impairment

shahbazigenomics/attest: attest 0.1.0

Amir Shahbazi — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23135720

First release of attest: audits an RNA-seq count matrix and its design before differential expression. It does not analyse expression. Each check gives a verdict (PERMITTED / CAUTION / NOT PERMITTED / UNKNOWN) with the evidence behind it, and names the claims the inputs could not answer. What it checks File reading (attest_file): how a count file was read, e.g. featureCounts annotation columns, gzip, separators, header lines Value scale: raw counts vs CPM / TPM / FPKM / log / normalised Identifiers: summary rows (htseq/STAR), Excel date corruption, mixed annotations Completeness: whether genes were filtered upstream Sample identity: sex inference from expression, duplicate libraries Design: estimability, and sample-sheet columns the formula leaves out Detectability (reported as scope, does not change the verdict): the smallest fold change DESeq2 could have detected, per gene Accepts a matrix, data.frame, SummarizedExperiment, DESeqDataSet, DGEList or tximport list. No input raises an error; malformed input returns a typed result with a reason. Install remotes::install_github("shahbazigenomics/attest@v0.1.0") MIT licensed. See NEWS.md for details.

Code and data for: Prognostic and Immune-Related Significance of DTL in Colorectal Cancer: an Integrative Multi-Omics Analysis

Jie Cheng — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23130960

Background and Objectives Colorectal cancer (CRC) represents one of the most prevalent malignancies affecting the digestive system. The Denticleless E3 ubiquitin protein ligase homolog (DTL) plays a pivotal role in substrate recognition during the process of protein degradation. Nevertheless, its specific functions and underlying mechanisms in the context of CRC have yet to be elucidated. Repository Contents (What is in this upload) This repository contains the R scripts and processed data supporting the findings of the associated manuscript.- scripts/: R scripts for reproducing the figures (Figure1 to Figure6) in the paper.- data/: Processed datasets and differential expression results based on the intersection of GEO datasets GSE36400 and GSE10950, as well as TCGA data.- README.md: Detailed instructions on software requirements, installation, and execution order.- LICENSE: MIT License. Methods Datasets from GEO (GSE36400 and GSE10950, taking the intersection) and TCGA were used, and clinical specimens were collected from Zhongnan Hospital of Wuhan University. DTL expression was analyzed through databases, qRT-PCR, Western blot, and immunohistochemistry. Prognostic and clinicopathological correlations were evaluated using Cox regression and Kaplan-Meier analyses. Biological functions were assessed via GO/KEGG enrichment and various assays in CRC cell lines. Single-cell transcriptomics and immune profiling were conducted to study DTL in the tumor microenvironment and its link to immunotherapy response. Results DTL was overexpressed in CRC tissues and cell lines, linked to poorer survival (HR = 2.0897, 95% CI: 1.0155 - 7.268, p < 0.05), and identified as an independent prognostic risk factor. Reducing DTL inhibited CRC cell proliferation, invasion, and migration. Analysis of publicly available single-cell RNA sequencing data demonstrated enrichment of DTL in the T-proliferative subset, potentially indicating the proliferative state of these cells. Elevated DTL expression correlated with increased T-cell infiltration and macrophage polarization. Furthermore, computational analyses suggested a possible association between high DTL expression and a diminished response to cytotoxic T-lymphocyte-associated protein 4 (CTLA-4) immunotherapy. Conclusion DTL is upregulated in CRC and associated with poor prognosis. It promotes proliferation, migration, and invasion in vitro. Its expression is associated with the tumor microenvironment and may be related to immunotherapy response, highlighting its potential as a prognostic and immune-related biomarker.

Fissioned from Mother V - AI, Digital Twins & Computational Longevity at the Limit - the volume expands longitudinal cohorts, deep phenotyping, multi-omics alignment, wearables, aging foundation models, personal state estimation, organ-specific twins, whole-person coupling, future validation, model drift, versioning, privacy, consent, and successor science into an 82-chapter Living Interactive research architecture. Its flagship Three-Coupling is: Longitudinal State x Twin Fidelity x Future Validation. The proposed LDTI heuristic links State Continuity, Twin Fidelity, Future Validation, and Uncertainty Calibration while retaining Data Drift, Model Staleness, and Context Loss as first-class risk terms. LDTI is a research instrument, not a law of nature or a clinical score. The book asks a deceptively difficult question: What must a longevity digital twin preserve across decades to remain biologically meaningful? A personal twin cannot be evaluated only by how detailed, personalized, multimodal, or visually coherent it appears. Its scientific value depends on whether biological state, functional meaning, measurement lineage, context, calibration, uncertainty, model versions, and future observations remain connected strongly enough for earlier claims to be reconstructed and challenged. The volume treats future reality as the strongest calibration partner for a personal twin. Predictions must remain challengeable. Historical states must remain reproducible. Model updates must not silently rewrite what was previously claimed. Sensor replacement must not be mistaken for biological change. Assay drift must not disappear inside a composite score. Population-level age prediction must not automatically be treated as within-person reversibility. And a digital twin must never acquire scientific authority merely because it has accumulated more data. All new indices are explicitly treated as proposed normalized research heuristics rather than validated medical scores. Each Gate is designed as an independent, falsifiable entrance into future work. Following the 200 Research Gates are 33 Answer Embryos. These are deliberately not final answers. Each Answer Embryo records a provisional answer structure, evidence that could strengthen it, observations that should weaken it, a design consequence if it survives, a falsification path, and a question for the next generation of researchers. The research lineage is: Gate -> Evidence -> Falsification -> Transformation -> Answer Embryo -> New Gate. The book ends with a simple discipline: Personalized Twin != Validated Future. A model should not inherit authority merely because it inherits data. Every generation must be allowed to test the map again.

Human liver organoid–multi-omics models for herb-induced liver injury: A Systematic review and evidence-to-translation appraisal

Fazil Ahmad — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.17605/osf.io/7grqw

ABSTRACT Background: Herb-induced liver injury (HILI) is an under-recognized cause of acute and chronic liver disease and remains challenging to diagnose and understand mechanistically. Conventional toxicology models inadequately represent human-specific biotransformation and cell-cell interactions. Human liver organoids (HLOs) in combination with multi-omics could help elucidate mechanisms of herbal hepatotoxicity, but the extent and translational potential of such evidence have not been systematically evaluated. Methods: A systematic review of articles that follow PRISMA 2020 guidelines was performed (registration:). Searches were conducted on PubMed/MEDLINE, Web of Science Core Collection, Scopus, and Google Scholar (200 results per search engine) from inception until 30 September 2026. Relevant studies that have investigated human liver organoids (hepatobiliary, multi-lineage, iPSC-derived or patient-derived organoids) after treatment with herbal medicines or phytochemicals using at least one omics technology were included. Two independent reviewers carried out screening and data extraction. Quality assessment was done using ToxRTool with additional specific domains for organoids and translational evidence rating from 1-5 for each study. Results: Of 1247 records identified, 412 duplicates were removed, 835 records were screened, and 153 full-text reports were assessed, identifying 37 eligible studies. Human liver organoid-multi-omics models captured hepatocellular, cholestatic, mitochondrial, inflammatory, fibrogenic, and exosome-mediated injury mechanisms. Transcriptomic, proteomic, metabolomic, spatial and single-cell analyses identified recurrent signatures involving oxidative/ER stress, mitochondrial dysfunction, bile-acid dysregulation, inflammatory signaling, and fibrosis. Independent biomarker validation, donor-specific risk prediction, and clinical validation was limited, suggesting predominantly exploratory-to-emerging translational evidence. Conclusions: Patient specific liver organoid multi omics approach provides a human relevant, mechanism-driven platform that links experimental toxicology with clinical hepatology. Aside from a broad discovery mechanism, it allows for early detection of diagnostically relevant markers, patient-specific risk assessment and mechanistically supported safety evaluation of herbal medicine. Organoid omics methods, through systematic standardization and clinical validation, could aid in precision diagnostics, clinical decision-making and risk evaluation for HILI. Keywords: Herb-induced liver injury (HILI); Multi-omics profiling; Human liver organoids (HLOs); Traditional herbal medicines; Precision toxicology.

Cardiomyocyte Membrane‐Camouflaged Spinach Nano‐Thylakoids Enhance Myocardial Energy Metabolism and Ameliorate Heart Failure

Haohui Fan, Bei Song, Chengyun Liu, Peiyu Zhong, Quan Zhou — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1002/smll.76087

BACKGROUND: Energy deficiency and mitochondrial dysfunction are key features of heart failure (HF), while current therapies only indirectly improve cardiac metabolism. We developed a biomimetic photoactivatable nanoplatform, cardiomyocyte membrane-camouflaged photosynthetic nano-thylakoids (MM-NTU), to enhance myocardial bioenergetics. METHODS AND RESULTS: Nano-thylakoids were isolated from spinach chloroplasts, downsized, and coated with cardiomyocyte membranes to improve cardiac association and retention. In transverse aortic constriction (TAC) rats, intramyocardial MM-NTU administration combined with red-light activation improved systolic function, reduced fibrosis, and attenuated ventricular remodeling. In angiotensin II-injured cardiomyocytes, MM-NTU stabilized mitochondrial membrane potential, increased ATP production, and suppressed hypertrophic and profibrotic markers. MM-NTU also modulated AMPK-related signaling and altered Pck1 and Adipoq expression, consistent with enhanced glucose utilization. Biodistribution and histological analyses showed favorable biocompatibility without evident major-organ toxicity. CONCLUSIONS: MM-NTU enhances ATP generation, glucose utilization, and mitochondrial energy metabolism, thereby improving myocardial bioenergetics and cardiac function under pressure overload.

Theory of Whole State Regulation under Mechanistic Incompleteness: Overall Research Framework and Theoretical Architecture

Bing Yuan — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22738474

Real world complex dynamic systems often require regulation before complete mechanistic models become available. This research framework asks why sustained regulation remains possible under mechanistic incompleteness and how it can be organised around a unified regulatory object through state representation, estimation, target comparison, intervention, feedback, and longitudinal trajectory management. The architecture comprises the A0 general theoretical framework and six core theoretical layers, spanning three object scales: complex dynamic systems, complex living systems, and the whole patient. Nineteen foundational papers connect the enabling conditions for sustained regulation with whole functional state, operational state representation, control architecture, medical translation, and pharmacotherapy systems. The central claim is that complete mechanistic models can improve regulation but are neither universally necessary for its initiation and continuation nor sufficient to guarantee stable, safe, effective, or optimal outcomes. Sustained regulation requires object continuity, observability relevant to decisions, evaluable goals, effective intervention, revision through feedback, and temporal and organisational continuity. Operational state representations and organisation around state deviations can reduce the complexity of representation and decision making required for a specific task without reducing the system's underlying complexity. In medicine, whole patient state provides a shared reference for coordinating disease specific treatment, functional changes, treatment burden, and patient preferences over time. Traditional Chinese medicine offers historical prototypes and empirical resources that require formalisation and scientific validation. The framework defines a coordinated research programme for computational implementation, clinical validation, testing across domains, and continuing revision. This record contains corresponding official English and Chinese editions in PDF and Word formats, sharing the DOI assigned to Version 1.1. It preserves the detailed theoretical architecture, paper positioning, research roadmap, terminology, and version history. Version 1.1 revision note Building on Version 1.0, this edition reorganises the foundational paper group from twenty to nineteen papers by incorporating the relevant content of Paper ID 2026.02.03 into Paper ID 2026.06.21 and Layer Two. It retains the eight core propositions while updating paper titles and roles, adding formal semantics and an accessible example linking five basic equations, and expanding discussion of interdisciplinary significance, empirical evaluation, theoretical boundaries, and failure conditions. The research roadmap and version documentation have been refined, the two language editions synchronised, and figures, tables, formatting, references, and citations corrected.

Estimating isotopic turnover rates using DEB theory

Emilie Cathelin, Sébastien Lefebvre, Carolina Giraldo — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ecolmodel.2026.111862

The isotopic turnover rate (λ) quantifies the rate at which an organism’s tissues are renewed. It is a key parameter in dietary studies as it governs predator-prey dynamics and by extension the broader food web. However, λ has historically been difficult to estimate, typically requiring a combination of experiments, empirical data and modelling approaches. Mechanistic models offer a promising alternative for estimating λ. Among these, the Dynamic Energy Budget (DEB) theory provides a particularly relevant framework for describing organism metabolism. In this study, we propose a formal expression of λ within the DEB framework, expressed as a function of length or body mass, temperature, and food intake. Model predictions show good agreement with λ values reported in the literature. By linking λ estimation to DEB theory, this approach provides a user-friendly formulation and enables the estimation of condition-dependent isotopic turnover rates across more than 7000 species across a wide range of taxa.

Multi-valvular burden score and one-year mortality in ICU patients undergoing echocardiography: an exploratory cohort study

Hasan Burak İşleyen, Sevil Tugrul Yavuz, S. Bulut, Fatih Kızkapan, Cevahir Alioglu — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s44156-026-00139-x

Abstract Background Multiple regurgitant lesions coexist in critically ill patients, yet cohort-level analyses typically represent each valve separately. We evaluated whether a Multi-Valvular Burden (MVB) score could summarize regurgitant involvement and whether its prognostic information differed from models retaining individual valve grades. Methods This retrospective cohort used MIMIC-IV v3.1 linked with MIMIC-IV-ECHO. Adults with an ICU admission and complete ordinal grading of tricuspid, mitral, and aortic regurgitation on an index echocardiogram within 72 h of ICU admission were included. MVB was the arithmetic sum of TR, MR, and AR grades (0–12). Cox models assessed 1-year all-cause mortality. Additional analyses compared MVB with separate valve-grade models, tested a high-burden definition requiring MVB > = 4 and at least two involved valves, used missing-aware AS/MS evidence proxies, and examined LVEF with structured RV-function classification. Results The cohort included 5,362 patients; 1,979 (36.9%) died within 1 year. Mortality increased from 24.6% at MVB 0 to 47.4% at MVB > = 4. After clinical adjustment, continuous MVB remained associated with mortality (HR 1.07 per point, 95% CI 1.05–1.09; P < 0.001), and MVB > = 4 was associated with higher mortality (HR 1.35, 95% CI 1.22–1.48; P < 0.001). The high-burden category predominantly reflected multivalve involvement: 2,206 of 2,257 patients with MVB > = 4 had at least two affected valves. A model with separate TR, MR, and AR grades showed similar discrimination to the MVB model (C-index 0.7585 vs. 0.7582). Incremental discrimination over the clinical model was small (C-index 0.7543 to 0.7582; delta 0.0039), although likelihood-ratio improvement was statistically significant (chi-square 50.61; P < 0.001). MVB remained associated with mortality in the missing-aware AS/MS sensitivity model (HR 1.07, 95% CI 1.05–1.09) and the LVEF/structured RV-function subset (HR 1.05, 95% CI 1.03–1.08). Conclusions MVB is an exploratory summary marker rather than an outcome-derived or mechanistic weighting system. Its near-identical discrimination to a model retaining individual valve grades and very modest incremental discrimination suggest that it is best viewed as a compact descriptive marker of broader cardiovascular burden within systemic critical illness, not as a stand-alone clinical decision tool or evidence of a causal pathway to mortality. Clinical trial number Not applicable. Trial registration Not applicable.

THE IMMANENT BACKEND OF REALITY Generative Rules, Hidden Structure, Emergence, Information, and the Architecture Beneath Observable Reality

33 — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23143435

THE IMMANENT BACKEND OF REALITY Generative Rules, Hidden Structure, Emergence, Information, and the Architecture Beneath Observable Reality Feng Cheng-en (33) x Starli THE IMMANENT BACKEND OF REALITY is a high-density flagship research monograph exploring a foundational question: What if observable reality is the surface expression of a deeper generative architecture? The book does not assume that the universe is a computer simulation, that an external supercomputer exists, or that reality is controlled by an outside programmer. Instead, it develops the concept of an “immanent backend”: a deeper architecture of rules, constraints, relations, state spaces, boundary conditions, causal structures, information flows, and generative processes that may belong to reality itself. The central distinction is therefore: Backend Hypothesis != Simulation Proof. A backend, in this framework, does not have to exist outside the universe. It may simply refer to structures beneath the level at which ordinary observable phenomena appear. The flagship Three-Coupling of the book is: Observable Phenomena x Generative Structure x Emergent Reality. Observable Phenomena includes measurements, records, events, and stable empirical regularities. Generative Structure includes candidate rules, state-transition systems, constraints, symmetries, boundary conditions, causal order, relational architecture, and state-space geometry. Emergent Reality includes robust higher-level structures that may remain real, causally useful, and scientifically indispensable even if they are not fundamental. The volume therefore rejects the assumption that deeper automatically means more real. One of its central principles is: Deeper Model != Deeper Reality. A deeper theory earns scientific value only when it generates new constraints, new counterfactuals, sharper predictions, discriminating experiments, or stronger cross-scale explanations. Across 82 chapters, the book explores epistemology, hidden structure, falsifiability, generative laws, boundary conditions, initial conditions, symmetry, conservation, causality, state spaces, emergence, universality, coarse-graining, renormalization, phase transitions, self-organization, complexity, information theory, computation, algorithmic complexity, quantum information, mathematical structure, geometry, topology, structural realism, quantum measurement, decoherence, entanglement, quantum fields, spacetime, gravity, cosmology, life, mind, agency, social reality, civilization, artificial intelligence, scientific discovery, causal representation learning, mechanistic interpretability, autonomous experimentation, anomaly discipline, and successor science. The volume introduces fourteen proposed comparative research heuristics, including the Immanent Backend Accessibility Index (IBAI), Generative Constraint Index, Explanatory Compression Index, Underdetermination Index, Falsifiability Reach Index, Emergence Alignment Index, Information-Constraint Index, Multi-Scale Integrity Index, Model Realizability Index, Observer-Horizon Index, Structural Adequacy Index, Causal Predictive Index, Anomaly Reproducibility Index, and Successor Readiness Index. These are research scaffolds, not laws of nature. Their purpose is to force hidden-structure claims to specify empirical constraints, predictive gains, uncertainty, rival explanations, falsifiers, and revision conditions. Seven geometric research models organize the conceptual architecture of the book: Phenomenon-to-Generator Stack,Constraint Manifold,Emergence Cone,Information-Structure Lattice,Equivalence-Class Surface,Observer-Interface Horizon,and Generative Architecture Atlas. These geometries are conceptual research spaces rather than literal physical metrics. They are designed to visualize admissible states, model degeneracy, causal bridges, inaccessible regions, observational equivalence, scale transitions, and the relationship between surface phenomena and candidate generative structures. A major theme of the book is underdetermination. Different hidden architectures may produce the same observable world. If two backend models generate identical empirical predictions, science may not yet be able to identify a unique underlying substrate. The appropriate result may therefore be an equivalence class of possible generators rather than a single metaphysical answer. This leads to one of the volume’s central questions: How much of a world’s generative architecture can an observer reconstruct while remaining inside that world? The book treats science as a form of internal reverse engineering. Human beings, instruments, and artificial intelligences do not stand outside reality. They infer its architecture from within through: Observation-> Pattern-> Model-> Prediction-> Intervention-> Falsification-> Revision. Artificial intelligence becomes especially important in this framework. Future AI systems may search enormous model spaces, discover latent representations, construct causal world models, design experiments, compare competing architectures, and detect structural regularities beyond unaided human reasoning. But AI also introduces a new danger: the ability to generate persuasive theories may grow faster than the ability to validate them. The book therefore argues that AI-assisted backend science must preserve uncertainty, model plurality, calibration, negative results, causal discrimination, and explicit claim boundaries. At the back of the volume are 200 Research Gates distributed across ten research zones: Epistemic Boundaries,Generative Rules and Constraints,Emergence and Effective Worlds,Information and Computation,Mathematics and Structure,Quantum and Measurement,Spacetime and Cosmology,Life, Mind, and Agency,AI Reverse Engineering,and Experimental and Successor Science. Each Research Gate is designed as an independent, falsifiable entrance capable of becoming a future research program, experiment, dissertation, or new flagship monograph. Following the Research Gates are 33 Answer Embryos. These are deliberately provisional. Each Answer Embryo includes conditions that could strengthen it, conditions that should weaken it, a design consequence if it survives, and a successor question. The research lineage is: Question-> Research-> Evidence-> Falsification-> Transformation-> Rebirth-> New Question. The book ultimately argues that the most mature approach to hidden structure is not to proclaim that the backend has already been found. It is to make the unknown increasingly testable. Perhaps reality has no machine behind it. Perhaps reality itself is the generative architecture. And perhaps the role of science is not to escape the world in order to see its source code, but to become increasingly capable of reconstructing the rules, constraints, relations, and emergent structures of reality from within. We do not stand outside reality. We infer its architecture from within. The task is not to name the hidden layer too early. The task is to build questions precise enough that reality can answer back.

Reproduction code and raw data for the manuscript Data-Driven Identification Protocol for Non-equilibrium Integrated Phases (NEIP): Resolution of Identifiability, Geometric Singularities, and Computational Bottlenecks (S. Takemoto, in preparation for submission to Physical Review E). This version supersedes the 2026-09-19 first draft; the corrections listed below apply to it and not to that draft. This deposit contains the software that produced every number in the manuscript, together with the raw result tables and the verbatim stdout log of each production run. No value in the manuscript was entered by hand: the result tables are written by the scripts, and verdict strings are computed from the measured quantities at run time rather than hard-coded. 22 driver, post-analysis and figure scripts; 10 result tables; 8 run logs. This package documents corrections applied to the manuscript after its first draft, and deliberately ships the negative results that motivated them: the confidence intervals were recomputed with moving blocks (B = 1000, L = 64, 20 seeds) and are 1.6-3.5 times wider than first reported; the k-nearest-neighbour estimator was withdrawn as a second line of evidence because its bias is a resolution floor (24.4x at the working point, 231x as the metric eigenvalue approaches 1e-3); multiplicity in the screening phase was controlled by a max-statistic correction (0.9275 -> 0.0225); the record-length scaling endpoint is 0.204, not 0.275, and is non-monotonic; and the original theoretical prediction was superseded and refuted by a factor of up to 2.7e4 by the corrected form. The run that failed to reach a verdict is included because it is the quantitative justification for the estimator redesign. Scope. The corrected theoretical expression was confirmed quantitatively on a separate controlled system with a uniform rotation rate. Only the structural form of the condition transfers to the many-body system studied in the manuscript; the rate-dependent scaling was not verified there. Requirements: Python >= 3.11, numpy, scipy, matplotlib. No compiled extensions and no external data are required. All runs are seeded. The full pipeline takes on the order of an hour on a laptop-class CPU; post16_theory_fit.py reproduces the theory verdicts in seconds from the shipped result table without re-running the 276 s simulation. DRIFT: A Gate Framework for Target-Relative Inference Under Model Uncertainty

Bernd von Mallinckrodt — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23129094

DRIFT ist ein vorgeschlagenes, zielrelatives Entscheidungsframework zur Beurteilung, ob eine statistische Inferenz für eine konkrete Zielaussage unter den vorgegebenen Annahmen und der verfügbaren Evidenz hinreichend unterstützt ist. Das Framework integriert fünf getrennte Prüfbereiche: Matching (M), Observability (O), Reliability (R), Invariance (I) und Nonstationarity (N). Die Ergebnisse werden ohne globalen Score in drei Zustände überführt: PASS, CONDITIONAL oder INSUFFICIENT. Diese Veröffentlichung enthält das wissenschaftliche Methodenmanuskript, DRIFT Frozen Protocol v1.1, die maschinenlesbare Spezifikation, Referenzimplementierung, vollständige Testsuite, Audit- und Änderungsprotokolle sowie die dokumentierte Identifizierbarkeitskorrektur A1. Die Korrektur betrifft die F1-Identifizierbarkeit bei einer Messwiederholung unter fest vorgegebener positiver Detektionsheterogenität und wurde vor jeder Entwicklungs- oder konfirmatorischen Datenerzeugung vorgenommen. Der konfirmatorische Benchmark wurde noch nicht durchgeführt. Es werden daher keine Aussagen über Überlegenheit, Gleichwertigkeit, Validierung oder empirische Nützlichkeit von DRIFT gemacht. Die Tests belegen die Konsistenz zwischen Spezifikation und Implementierung, nicht die wissenschaftliche Wirksamkeit des Verfahrens. Der zukünftige Benchmark vergleicht DRIFT familienweise mit starken Vergleichsverfahren, darunter ein Equal-Information-Comparator, und bewertet Fehlbehauptungen und Fehlverweigerungen über eine vorab festgelegte Verlustfunktion. Die wissenschaftliche Kernlogik ist zugleich als Grundlage für eine spätere Umsetzung in BenchEWS Studio 3.0 vorgesehen. Diese Softwareübersetzung ist Zukunftsarbeit und nicht Teil der vorliegenden Evidenz. SchlagwörterDRIFT; statistische Inferenz; Modellunsicherheit; Posterior-Predictive-Checks; Identifizierbarkeit; praktische Identifizierbarkeit; partielle Identifikation; Zuverlässigkeit; Robustheit; Invarianz; Nichtstationarität; Abstention; Reject Option; Modellvalidierung; Reproduzierbarkeit; Preregistrierung; Verifikation und Validierung; Unsicherheitsquantifizierung; Entscheidungsunterstützung; BenchEWS Studio 3.0 English DescriptionDRIFT is a proposed target-relative decision framework for assessing whether a statistical inference is sufficiently supported for a specific target claim under declared assumptions and available evidence. The framework integrates five distinct assessment domains: Matching (M), Observability (O), Reliability (R), Invariance (I), and Nonstationarity (N). Without computing a global score, the framework maps the evidence to one of three states: PASS, CONDITIONAL, or INSUFFICIENT. This release contains the scientific methods manuscript, DRIFT Frozen Protocol v1.1, the machine-readable specification, reference implementation, complete test suite, audit and amendment records, and the documented identifiability correction A1. The correction concerns F1 identifiability with a single observation occasion under fixed positive detection heterogeneity and was implemented before any development or confirmatory data were generated or accessed. The confirmatory benchmark has not yet been run. Accordingly, this release makes no claim of superiority, equivalence, validation, or empirical usefulness of DRIFT. The test suite establishes consistency between the specification and implementation; it does not establish scientific effectiveness. The future benchmark will compare DRIFT separately across benchmark families against strong comparators, including an equal-information comparator, and will evaluate false assertions and false refusals using a prespecified loss framework. The scientific core is also intended to provide the basis for a future implementation in BenchEWS Studio 3.0. That software translation is future work and is not part of the evidence presented in this release. KeywordsDRIFT; statistical inference; model uncertainty; posterior predictive checks; identifiability; practical identifiability; partial identification; reliability; robustness; invariance; nonstationarity; abstention; reject option; model validation; reproducibility; preregistration; verification and validation; uncertainty quantification; decision support; BenchEWS Studio 3.0

Can We Know the Whole from Within? The Embedded Observer Problem and the Limits of Global-State Identification

Kristijan Kozic — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23131958

We study global-state identification by an observer embedded within a system and restricted to finite-budget, geometrically connected observations. The central question is whether information that is collectively sufficient to identify a global state can necessarily be integrated by a single admissible observer. For finite-field linear models, we characterize accessible information through rooted connected supports and the cycle space of the underlying graph. This leads to an exact characterization of the collective identification threshold in terms of rooted cycle access. For an n × n square grid rooted at a corner, we prove that the collective identification threshold is 2n + 2, whereas complete integration of the cycle space by a single rooted observer requires 2n(n + 1) observations. Consequently, the ratio between the single-observer and collective thresholds is exactly n and therefore grows without bound with system size. The result establishes an explicit separation between collective global identifiability and single-observer integration. We additionally derive an asymptotic integration-gap bound and extend the framework to additively noisy observations on finite Abelian groups, where the geometric separation admits a corresponding interpretation through Blackwell comparison of statistical experiments.

Project MASK (Math Ay Sikaping Kompyutin): A Numeracy Intervention for Grade 7 Students

Gim B. Sadol, John Reck L. Niepes, Keeven Cozter S. Antique — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.67903/ijroms0480

Numeracy is a fundamental competency for academic learning and everyday decision-making, yet difficulties in foundational numeracy remain a concern among learners entering secondary education. This study addressed the need for localized evidence on school-based numeracy interventions for Grade 7 learners identified as non-numerate. It evaluated changes in the numeracy performance of Grade 7 learners following the implementation of Project MASK (Math Ay Sikaping Kompyutin) at Santiago Integrated National High School during School Year 2024–2025. The study employed a pre-experimental action research evaluation using a one-group pretest-posttest design involving 195 Grade 7 learners identified as non-numerate through the Division Numeracy Assessment (DNA). Project MASK was implemented over 12 weeks through 36 sessions organized into four phases: Monitoring and Assessment, Adaptive Instruction, Supportive Learning, and Knowledge Application. Pre-test and post-test scores were analyzed using descriptive statistics and the Wilcoxon signed-rank test because the paired difference scores were not normally distributed. The mean score increased from 1.98 (SD = 0.894) in the pre-test to 7.62 (SD = 1.140) in the post-test, representing an observed mean increase of 5.64 points. The difference between the paired assessments was statistically significant (W = 0.00, p < .001), with a rank-biserial correlation of −1.00, indicating that all observed paired differences favored the post-test. The findings demonstrate statistically significant improvement in numeracy performance following implementation. However, because the study used a one-group design without a comparison group, the observed improvement cannot be attributed solely to Project MASK. Controlled or quasi-experimental research is therefore needed to determine the intervention’s causal contribution.

Inferential Standing Under Evidential Preservation: Non-Inheritance and Projection-Relative Non-Identifiability of Continuing Inferential Warrant

Sandra Škrinjar — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23137645

This paper examines whether preservation of an evidential state is sufficient to preserve the standing of the same inference drawn from that state. It introduces the Inferential Warrant Non-Inheritance Theorem (IWNI), a formal result showing that even where the warranted anchor, comparison basis, measurement accuracy, measured relation, proposition identity, historical inferential warrant, operative standing of the warrant specification, and absence of sufficient independent positive and negative current bases are held fixed, current inferential standing need not be identifiable from the preserved evidential projection without information establishing current coverage. The result is established through a paired determination-state construction in which two admissible states share the same preserved evidential projection while differing in current coverage and therefore in current inferential standing. The paper distinguishes preservation of evidence from preservation of what that evidence is currently warranted to establish, while explicitly avoiding claims of temporal decay, universal revalidation, substantive truth criteria, or priority over existing work on non-identifiability, defeasible inference, measurement invariance, transportability, and related fields. The analysis is developed as a formalization of a non-inheritance boundary within the Škrinjar Structural Architecture (SSA). Central boundary: PRESERVED EVIDENTIAL STATE DOES NOT ENTAIL PRESERVED INFERENTIAL STANDING.

Reference Transformation Evaluation Principle (RTEP) A Domain-Independent Systemic Framework for Reference-Dependent Evaluation of a Focal State

Shamiul Hoque Shan — 2026-10-04 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23141351

The Reference Transformation Evaluation Principle (RTEP) is proposed as a general systems-theoretic framework for representing situations in which the evaluation of a focal state changes because the reference structure against which that state is evaluated is selected, weighted, reframed, or otherwise transformed. The framework explicitly separates the focal state F, evaluation criterion C, reference structure \mathcal{R}_{N}, transformation operator T, transformation mechanism M, transformation parameters \theta, evaluation function E, and time t. The canonical formulation is followed by The framework therefore treats reference transformation as an explicit operator rather than as an implicit contextual variable. Thirty synthetic cases are organized into three layers: elementary reference-dependent evaluation, systemic reference structures, and generalized mathematical forms. The cases cover relational, numerical, academic, professional, organizational, risk, network, adaptive, nonlinear, and perturbative settings. Formal propositions establish null-transformation invariance and a Lipschitz-type upper bound on evaluation displacement under bounded reference transformation. The manuscript further specifies absolute and relative reference modes, weighted and multiple-reference systems, dynamic systems, nonlinear evaluation, vector and matrix-valued states, falsification conditions, identifiability constraints, and methodological safeguards against post-selection bias. The framework is presented as a proposed domain-independent mechanism rather than as an empirically established universal law. Mathematical derivations establish properties of the proposed model; the synthetic cases illustrate its operational scope; empirical universality remains an open testable hypothesis.

Computational systems immunology and multi-scale modeling for the design of a Multi-Epitope Vaccine (MEV) against emerging multidrug-resistant Klebsiella michiganensis

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Rasha Assad Assiri, Fayez M. Saleh, Sher Muhammad, Emmanuel O. Fenibo, Tonderayi Matambo — 2026-10-03 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s10123-026-00903-3

Klebsiella michiganensis, an emerging multidrug-resistant member of the Klebsiella oxytoca complex, has become an important opportunistic pathogen responsible for nosocomial infections, including bacteremia and ventilator-associated pneumonia. Owing to its rapidly evolving resistance to carbapenems, new preventive strategies are urgently needed. This study presents a computational systems immunology framework integrating subtractive proteomics, reverse vaccinology, and immunoinformatics to design a multi-epitope vaccine (MEV) against K. michiganensis strain ATCC 8724. From the complete proteome (5,483 proteins), four proteins associated with the bacterial envelope and LPS-related processes were prioritized as computational vaccine targets: the outer-membrane β-barrel proteins BamA and LptD, the periplasmic LPS transport component LptA, and the outer-membrane lipoprotein LolB. These were selected as non-homologous and antigenic candidates. Cytotoxic T-lymphocyte, helper T-lymphocyte, and B-cell epitopes derived from these proteins were evaluated for antigenicity, toxicity, allergenicity, and global HLA population coverage. The selected epitopes were assembled using AAY, GPGPG, and KK linkers with the Mycobacterium tuberculosis 50 S ribosomal protein L7/L12 as an adjuvant to construct a 386-amino-acid chimeric MEV. Structural modeling and refinement showed 95.6% residues in favored regions of the Ramachandran plot. Molecular docking with the TLR1 component (chain B) of the TLR1-TLR2 heterodimer predicted a favorable interaction (ΔG = - 14.8 kcal/mol), while a 100-ns molecular dynamics simulation suggested maintenance of the overall docked-complex architecture. C-ImmSim predicted increases in immunoglobulin and cytokine levels consistent with potential activation of humoral and cellular immunity. Codon optimization (CAI = 0.94, GC = 50%) supported predicted expression feasibility in E. coli. This computationally designed MEV represents a candidate for future experimental evaluation against K. michiganensis, requiring further experimental validation.

HPV vaccination and cervical cancer deaths: from incidence reduction to mortality prevention

Mohammed Alkhanafsa, Jamil Wafi, Soud Deek, Ahmed abdalhadi — 2026-10-03 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s44272-026-00074-2

Abstract Persistent infection with carcinogenic human papillomavirus (HPV) types causes the great majority of cervical cancers. For more than a decade, randomized trials, surveillance studies, and national registry analyses have demonstrated reductions in vaccine-type HPV infection, high-grade cervical precancer, and invasive cervical cancer following HPV vaccination. Direct evidence regarding cervical cancer mortality has emerged more recently because cohorts vaccinated in early adolescence first had to reach ages at which cervical cancer deaths became observable. A 2026 population-based analysis from England reported no cervical cancer deaths among the most highly vaccinated women aged 20 to 24 years during 2020 to 2024, with substantial mortality reductions also observed in older vaccinated cohorts, although uncertainty increased with age. This narrative review synthesizes the biological and epidemiologic evidence linking HPV vaccination to mortality prevention, critically appraises the England mortality study and its age-stratified findings, considers limitations related to cervical screening and the modeled counterfactual, and discusses implications for cervical cancer elimination, including relevance to Qatar and the wider Eastern Mediterranean region. Current evidence supports early adolescent HPV vaccination as a central cancer-prevention intervention, while continued cervical screening, timely treatment, high and equitable vaccine coverage, and long-term surveillance remain essential. The available mortality evidence is compelling at the population-program level but should not be interpreted as an individual-level randomized estimate of vaccine effectiveness against cervical cancer death.

Immunogenic effects of a combined recombinant protein formulation based on Brucella Omp19, Omp2b, Omp31, and BP26

Mixue Guo, Siyu Hou, Huqiang Dong, Peijiao Wu, Xiaoli Qin — 2026-10-03 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12866-026-05708-y

Brucellosis is a neglected zoonotic disease for which no approved human vaccine is currently available. For Brucella , a typical intracellular pathogen, the success of recombinant protein vaccine development largely depends on whether it can effectively induce immune responses with a strong Th1-associated cellular component. This study aimed to systematically compare the immunological characteristics of single-antigen immunization with rOmp19, rOmp2b, rOmp31, or rBP26 and a four-antigen combined immunization strategy under a uniform CpG adjuvant regimen, and to evaluate the relative advantages of the combined strategy. Female C57BL/6N mice aged 6–8 weeks were randomly assigned to a PBS control group, a CpG control group, four single-antigen immunization groups, and a four-antigen combined immunization group (Combo + CpG). Mice were immunized three times by three-point subcutaneous injection in the abdomen at 2-week intervals. Serum and splenocytes were collected at weeks 1, 2, 4, and 14 after the final immunization. Macrophage polarization, antigen-specific antibody responses, T-cell activation and proliferation, cytokine secretion, and memory T-cell phenotypes were systematically evaluated by ELISA, flow cytometry, and ELISPOT. Functional activity and safety were further assessed through serum complement-dependent bactericidal assays, serum biochemical analysis, and splenic histopathological observation. Under the same CpG adjuvant regimen, all recombinant protein immunization groups induced humoral and cellular immune responses to varying degrees. Compared with single-antigen immunization, the Combo + CpG group showed a more coordinated enhancement across multiple key indicators, including more pronounced changes in macrophage-associated CD11c and CD206 phenotypes during the early and intermediate stages after immunization, higher levels of total IgG and certain IgG subclass responses, more significant activation and proliferation of CD4 + and CD8 + T cells after antigen restimulation, stronger IFN-γ-associated cellular immune responses, and more prominent complement-dependent bactericidal activity in vitro. Meanwhile, the combined immunization group also showed relative advantages in certain CD4 + central memory T-cell (TCM) and CD8 + effector memory T-cell (TEM) subsets. No obvious abnormalities were observed in serum biochemical parameters or splenic histology in any immunization group, suggesting that no detectable systemic toxicity was produced under the conditions of this study. Although the four-antigen combined immunization strategy did not outperform the single-antigen strategies in every individual parameter, it demonstrated a more stable overall advantage in Th1-related cellular immunity, selected humoral immune indicators, in vitro functional activity, and overall immunological coordination. These findings identify the four-antigen combination as an immunogenic formulation warranting further optimization and evaluation in in vivo Brucella challenge models.

**From the "Triple Filter" to the Cognitive Contract: Applying Socratic Information Ethics to Digital and Social Media Environments**

Sudhakar Geruganti — 2026-10-03 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23115360

--- ## Alternative Titles 1. **Truth, Goodness, Utility: Reviving the Socratic Triple Filter for the Age of Misinformation**2. **The Examined Word: Socratic Ethics as a Framework for Digital Citizenship and Social Media Literacy**3. **Before You Share: The Socratic Triple Filter and the Fight Against Disinformation in the Digital Public Sphere**4. **From the Agora to the Algorithm: Socratic Information Ethics in the Age of Social Media**5. **Questioning the Feed: How Socratic Philosophy Can Combat Misinformation, Cyberbullying, and Information Overload**6. **The Socratic Digital Contract: A Three-Level Framework for Ethical Communication on Social Media**7. **Wisdom in the Age of Virality: Applying the Socratic Method to Digital and Social Media Information** --- ## Subtitles 1. **A Three-Level Framework Integrating Individual Ethics, Platform Design, and Digital Citizenship Education**2. **Combating Disinformation, Cyberbullying, and Information Overload Through Classical Wisdom**3. **Truth, Goodness, and Utility as Pillars of Ethical Communication in the Digital Public Sphere**4. **From the Ancient Agora to the Algorithmic Feed: Socratic Method as a Practical Tool for Digital Literacy**5. **The Socratic Triple Filter and the Cognitive Crisis of the Digital Age: Applications, Limitations, and Future Directions**6. **Rethinking Information Ethics for Social Media: A Socratic Approach to Verification, Benevolence, and Utility** --- ## Detailed Description ### Overview This research paper examines the application of the Socratic "Triple Filter"—Truth, Goodness, and Utility—as an ethical and practical framework for navigating the information crisis of digital and social media environments. Drawing on a 2026 survey revealing that 93.82% of university students encountered suspected false information on social media and over 60% were misled by it, the paper argues that individual-level ethical tools are indispensable complements to platform-level technological governance and systemic digital citizenship education. The study bridges ancient Greek philosophy with contemporary challenges such as disinformation, cyberbullying, opinion polarization, and information overload, proposing a "Socratic Digital Information Contract" as a three-level intervention framework. ### Problem Statement Social media platforms have created an information ecosystem characterized by unprecedented speed, scale, and low barriers to entry. While this democratization of information has benefits, it has also produced systemic harms: disinformation spreads faster than verified facts, outrage generates more engagement than nuance, and attention is harvested as a commodity. Existing responses—fact-checking, content moderation, algorithmic detection—are necessary but insufficient. They operate at the platform level, often opaquely, and fail to address the cognitive and ethical dimensions of individual information behavior. This paper identifies a critical gap: the absence of a simple, memorable, and philosophically grounded framework that individuals can internalize to guide their own communication practices. The Socratic Triple Filter fills this gap. ### Objectives 1. To articulate the philosophical foundations of the Socratic Triple Filter, including its roots in Socratic virtue ethics, Platonic epistemology, and the principle of epistemic responsibility.2. To map the three filters—Truth, Goodness, Utility—onto specific digital challenges: disinformation, cyberbullying, and information overload.3. To analyze the limitations of individual-level ethical frameworks, particularly the "double ignorance" problem and the risk of victim-blaming.4. To evaluate the complementary roles of platform design (Socratic nudges, AI-assisted verification) and digital citizenship education.5. To propose a "Socratic Digital Information Contract" as a comprehensive, three-level framework for ethical communication in the digital age.6. To identify directions for future empirical research on the effectiveness of Socratic interventions. ### Key Findings **The Triple Filter Mechanism**: The Socratic method requires information to pass three sequential tests before transmission: Is it true? Is it good? Is it useful? This conjunctive structure implies that silence is the default ethical position; speech requires justification. The three filters align with the cardinal Greek virtues of Wisdom, Temperance, and Justice. **Alignment with Digital Literacy Frameworks**: The first filter (Truth) corresponds directly to UNESCO's Media and Information Literacy (MIL) competencies, which position verification as a core skill. The Triple Filter translates abstract competencies into a memorable ethical imperative: do not transmit what you have not verified. **The "Double Ignorance" Problem**: A critical limitation emerges when users lack both knowledge and awareness of their ignorance. Algorithmic echo chambers and emotional engagement metrics exacerbate this condition, making Socratic questioning ineffective for certain populations. This finding underscores the necessity of complementing individual ethics with platform design and education. **Platform-Level Interventions**: The paper proposes "Socratic nudges"—non-intrusive prompts displayed before sharing content—as a behavioral economics-informed approach to reducing misinformation spread. Experimental evidence suggests such prompts can reduce sharing of false content without resorting to censorship. **Educational Integration**: Digital citizenship education should incorporate the Triple Filter as a core competency, fostering metacognitive awareness and intellectual humility. This addresses the "double ignorance" problem by helping learners recognize the limits of their own knowledge. **Case Study Applications**: The paper applies the Triple Filter to two contemporary cases—COVID-19 vaccine misinformation and political polarization on Twitter/X—demonstrating how the framework can guide individual decisions to reject and refrain from sharing harmful content. ### Argument and Contribution The central argument is that the Socratic Triple Filter offers a timeless yet urgently relevant framework for navigating the cognitive crisis of the digital age. While the Socratic method was born in face-to-face dialogue in the ancient Athenian agora, its translation into the digital public sphere is both possible and necessary. The paper contributes a unique synthesis of classical philosophy, digital literacy theory, and platform governance studies, proposing the "Socratic Digital Information Contract" as a comprehensive framework that integrates: - **Individual level**: Ethical self-regulation through habitual application of the Triple Filter.- **Platform level**: Technological empowerment through Socratic nudges and transparent verification tools.- **Educational level**: Systemic digital citizenship education that cultivates critical thinking, metacognition, and intellectual humility. The paper positions the Triple Filter not as a panacea but as a necessary component of a multi-level strategy, complementing—not replacing—platform regulation, media accountability, and structural reform. ### Methodology The paper employs a qualitative, interdisciplinary approach combining:- **Textual and Philosophical Analysis**: Close reading of Socratic dialogues (Plato's *Apology*) and secondary scholarship on Socratic ethics and epistemology.- **Literature Review**: Synthesis of research on misinformation, cyberbullying, digital literacy, and behavioral economics.- **Applied Ethics**: Mapping the ancient framework onto contemporary digital challenges through case studies.- **Conceptual Synthesis**: Developing the "Socratic Digital Information Contract" as a novel framework for ethical communication in digital environments. ### Target Audience and Relevance This paper is intended for philosophers, educators, media scholars, digital ethics researchers, platform designers, and communication professionals. It is highly relevant to ongoing public debates about media literacy, platform regulation, the ethics of AI-driven content moderation, and the cultivation of critical thinking in the digital age. The Socratic framework's simplicity and cross-cultural resonance make it particularly valuable for global audiences seeking practical tools to combat misinformation and foster healthier digital public spheres. ### One-Sentence Summary This paper argues that the Socratic Triple Filter—Truth, Goodness, Utility—provides a timeless and practical ethical framework for combating disinformation, cyberbullying, and information overload in digital and social media environments, and proposes a three-level "Socratic Digital Information Contract" integrating individual ethics, platform design, and digital citizenship education to address the cognitive crisis of the digital age. --- ## Suggested Structural Outline for the Paper 1. **Introduction**: The digital information crisis and the relevance of Socratic ethics2. **The Philosophical Foundations of the Triple Filter**: Truth, Goodness, Utility; Socratic virtues; epistemic responsibility3. **Mapping the Triple Filter onto Digital Challenges**: Disinformation, cyberbullying, information overload4. **Case Studies**: COVID-19 vaccine misinformation; political polarization on Twitter/X5. **Limitations and the "Double Ignorance" Problem**: The limits of individual ethics6. **The Socratic Digital Information Contract**: A three-level framework (individual, platform, educational)7. **Future Research Directions**: Empirical studies, cross-cultural analyses, AI integration8. **Conclusion**: The examined word is worth speaking; the unexamined word is not9. **References** ### Alternative Titles1. **Truth, Goodness, and Utility: Reviving the Socratic Triple Filter for Modern Communication**2. **Before You Speak: The Socratic Method as an Antidote to Misinformation and Gossip**3. **The Three Gates of S

mRNA-LNP vaccines targeting conserved virulence factors elicit protective immunity against lethal Legionella pneumophila infection

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Heng Xia, Yun-Ru He, Haowei Qiu, Jun Xu, Yuan-Tong He — 2026-10-03 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12951-026-05082-x

Legionnaires’ disease (LD), caused by the intracellular pathogen Legionella pneumophila , imposes a substantial global health burden, yet no vaccine has been clinically approved. Here, we develop mRNA-LNP vaccines encoding conserved virulence factors Pal, PilE, and FlaA. In BALB/c mice, immunization expands T follicular helper and germinal center B cells, elicits high-titer IgG, and induces potent IFN-γ⁺ Th1-type responses, with no observed systemic toxicity. In a lethal challenge model (2 × 10 8 CFU in susceptible A/J mice), monovalent FlaA-LNP confers superior protection with 100% survival and sterilizing immunity, whereas PilE-LNP is less protective. Following sublethal challenge, spatiotemporal profiling in A/J mice reveals systemic CD8⁺ effector responses. At the pulmonary mucosa, more protective vaccine formulations (Pal-, FlaA-, and MIX-LNP) expand CD4⁺ and CD8⁺ memory T cells; yet within the tissue-resident memory T (TRM) subset, the increase is selectively restricted to the CD8⁺. This CD8⁺ TRM enrichment, together with increased alveolar macrophages and mucosal sIgA responses, correlates with protective efficacy and defines the local immune landscape. In the bone marrow, plasma cell fate is antigen-dependent: FlaA is associated with acute short-lived plasma cell bursts, whereas PilE is required for long-lived plasma cell seeding. Correlation analyses further link the Tfh–GCB axis and local lung microenvironment (including memory T cells, alveolar macrophages, and mucosal sIgA) to protective efficacy. Collectively, these findings demonstrate that mRNA-LNP vaccines orchestrate a multilayered immune defense against L. pneumophila , coupling acute humoral recall, mucosal CD8⁺ TRM establishment, and antigen-dependent plasma cell fate, providing a translatable mechanistic framework for next-generation vaccines. It schematically summarizes antigen selection from Legionella pneumophila, mRNA-LNP vaccine construction, immunization and immune responses, lethal intratracheal challenge protection, and the mechanistic immune landscape in the lung, spleen, and bone marrow

A qualitative study of the 2025 mpox epidemic in Sierra Leone as a multidimensional social phenomenon

Eric Nzirakaindi Ikoona, Lucy Namulemo, Mary Magdalene Sinnah, Mohamed Alex Vandi, Foday Sahr — 2026-10-03 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s44155-026-00500-9

Mpox (formerly known as monkeypox) caused 5442 confirmed cases and 60 deaths in Sierra Leone in 2025. Epidemiological and genomic studies describe its scale and lineage, but not how people governed, interpreted, lived, and survived it. We conducted an interpretive qualitative study in Western Area Urban and Port Loko District. Purposive maximum-variation sampling included survivors, caregivers, clinicians, surveillance and contact-tracing staff, community health workers, community leaders, and policy actors. Fifty-two people completed in-depth interviews. Thirty-eight different people joined six focus group discussions, yielding 90 unique participants. Data collection occurred in Krio, Temne, Mende, and English. We combined the Framework Method with reflexive thematic analysis across six interacting dimensions: structural, institutional, cultural, relational, subjective, and systemic. Material conditions constrained isolation and shifted control costs toward households. Emergency institutions made cases visible, but bed scarcity and frontline discretion shaped facility care. Visible lesions acquired moral meanings linked to sex, dirt, punishment, human immunodeficiency virus (HIV), and Ebola memory. Relationships carried infection, care, disclosure, stigma, and trusted information. Survivors described pain, shame, income loss, altered intimacy, and difficult reintegration. Ebola supplied the dominant bodily analogy, while COVID-19 shaped accounts of restrictions, vaccination, and response fatigue. System limits included workforce pressure, vaccine scarcity, and prolonged needs after clinical recovery. Mpox control required diagnosis, isolation, and vaccination, but technical measures worked through social conditions. Effective response therefore needs livelihood support, safe household care, trusted communication, stigma reduction, survivor follow-up, and transparent resource allocation.

Real-world safety of dengue vaccines in Brazil: A pharmacovigilance study

Matheus Negri Boschiero, Nathália Mariana Santos Sansone, Fernando Augusto Lima Marson — 2026-10-03 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.vaccine.2026.129178

BACKGROUND: The expansion of dengue vaccination strategies in Brazil, including the introduction of the Butantan dengue vaccine (DV), has reinforced the importance of post-marketing safety surveillance. We aimed to characterize reporting patterns of adverse events following immunization (AEFIs) and immunization errors associated with dengue vaccines in Brazil. METHODS: We conducted a nationwide retrospective observational study using records of AEFIs associated with dengue vaccines reported to the Brazilian Ministry of Health through June 2026. AEFIs were classified as serious or non-serious according to national surveillance criteria, and immunization errors were analyzed separately. RESULTS: Among 47,666 individuals with reported AEFIs or immunization errors following dengue vaccination, 525 (1.1%) experienced at least one serious AEFI, 43,043 (90.3%) reported non-serious AEFIs, and 4098 (8.6%) were classified as immunization errors. Serious AEFIs were uncommon across vaccine platforms. Among recipients of Butantan-DV, the most frequent serious AEFIs were skin manifestations, central nervous system symptoms, and myalgia or arthralgia. Among recipients of other dengue vaccines, skin manifestations, anaphylaxis or bronchospasm, and respiratory symptoms predominated. Non-serious AEFIs were mainly exanthema, headache, and pruritus. Immunization errors were primarily related to inappropriate age at vaccination, shortened intervals between doses, and incorrect routes of administration. Reporting rates increased during the expansion of dengue vaccination activities in 2026. CONCLUSIONS: In this nationwide pharmacovigilance study, dengue vaccines demonstrated a favorable safety profile, with serious adverse events accounting for only a small proportion of reported events. These findings support the implementation of dengue vaccination programs while reinforcing the importance of ongoing surveillance to identify rare safety signals and reduce preventable immunization errors.

Sphingosomes: A Novel Lipoidal Vesicular Drug Delivery System – A Review

Dr. Sachitanand Biradar*, Sai Kondlyade, Vaishnavi Dhore — 2026-10-03 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23119843

Sphingosomes are emerging lipoidal vesicular drug-delivery systems composed primarily of sphingolipids, particularly sphingomyelin, frequently combined with cholesterol and other formulation components. Their sphingolipid-rich bilayer provides a relatively ordered membrane structure that can support drug retention, membrane stability and controlled release. This review summarizes the development, composition, structural features, preparation approaches, drug-loading mechanisms, characterization parameters, factors influencing formulation performance, routes of administration, pharmaceutical applications, recent advances, clinical status, challenges and future perspectives of sphingosomes. Sphingosomes can accommodate hydrophilic drugs within the aqueous core and lipophilic drugs within the lipid bilayer, while amphiphilic molecules may be distributed at the membrane–aqueous interface. Passive and active loading approaches can be used depending on drug properties and formulation design. Common preparation approaches include thin-film hydration, reverse-phase evaporation, ethanol injection, freeze–thaw processing and microfluidic preparation. Important quality attributes include vesicle size, polydispersity index, morphology, zeta potential, lamellarity, drug-loading capacity, entrapment efficiency, drug content, in-vitro release and storage stability. Sphingosomes have potential applications in oral, topical, transdermal, ocular, intravenous, pulmonary and nasal delivery, and have been investigated for anticancer, antifungal and antiviral therapy as well as delivery of vaccines, proteins, peptides and nucleic acids. Recent research directions include targeted and PEGylated systems, stimuli-responsive delivery, combination therapy, microfluidic manufacturing, AI-assisted formulation optimization and theranostic applications. Despite these advantages, scale-up, cost, sterilization, long-term stability, reproducibility, regulatory requirements and limited clinical translation remain important barriers. Further standardized characterization, safety evaluation, scalable manufacturing and clinical studies are required to establish sphingosomes as clinically. useful drug-delivery platforms

Association between introduction of 13-valent pneumococcal conjugate vaccine and burden of hospitalized adult pneumococcal disease in South Korea – a population-based retrospective database study

Joon Young Song, Eun Hwa Choi, Young June Choe, See‐Hwee Yeo, Dony Patel — 2026-10-03 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12889-026-29058-6

Abstract Background Information on the burden of pneumococcal disease and its association with vaccination in South Korea remains limited. This study aims to examine the incidence rate, case fatality rate, healthcare resource utilization and costs of pneumococcal disease in adults before and after the introduction of 13-valent pneumococcal conjugate vaccine (PCV13) into South Korea’s childhood immunization program (CIP) in 2014. Methods Episodes of eligible adults aged at least 18 years hospitalized with invasive pneumococcal disease or pneumonia between 1 January 2010 and 31 December 2019 were extracted from South Korea’s National Health Information Database. The Korean version of the International Classification of Diseases codes were used for case identification. Study findings were compared between pre-CIP (2010–2013) and post-CIP (2015–2019) periods. Changes in the level and slope change of incidence rates in the post-CIP period were assessed with interrupted time series analyses and reported using incidence rate ratio (IRR). Healthcare resource utilization components consisted of length of hospitalization, and number of inpatient, outpatient and emergency department visits. Direct costs were calculated. Results There were a total of 1,682 invasive pneumococcal disease episodes and 8,635 pneumonia episodes included in this study. Overall incidence rates for invasive pneumococcal disease and pneumonia were 0.4 and 2.1 episodes per 100,000 person-years, respectively. For invasive pneumococcal disease, there were no significant changes in the level of incidence rate (IRR = 1.26; 95% confidence interval [CI]: [0.99, 1.59]) and slope of incidence rate (IRR = 0.97; 95% CI: [0.93, 1.01]) in the post-CIP period. For pneumonia, level of incidence rate was significantly higher in the post-CIP period than in the pre-CIP period (IRR = 1.92; 95% CI: [1.51, 2.42]), but no difference in slopes were observed between the two time periods (IRR = 1.02; 95% CI: [1.00, 1.04]). Case fatality rate was 14.9% for invasive pneumococcal disease and 3.0% for pneumonia. The average total costs per pneumonia episode was higher in the post-CIP period (4.1 vs. 3.1 million Korean Won). Disease burden was generally higher in patients ≥ 65 years. Conclusions Incidence of invasive pneumococcal disease remained stable while incidence and economic burden of pneumonia in adults had increased after PCV13 introduction into South Korea’s CIP. Use of PCV13 and factors such as increased surveillance may explain the observed results.
